  :::

- **107** [\[Repealed, SOR/96-437, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Lighting Systems and Reflective Devices (Standard 108)]{.HTitleText2} {#lighting-systems-and-reflective-devices-standard-108 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [Passenger Cars, Multi-purpose Passenger Vehicles, Trucks, Trailers and Buses]{.HTitleText3} {#passenger-cars-multi-purpose-passenger-vehicles-trucks-trailers-and-buses .SchedHeadL3}

- - **108** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle, truck, trailer and bus shall conform to Technical
    Standards Document No. 108, Lamps, Reflective Devices, and
    Associated Equipment (TSD 108), as amended from time to time.

  ##### [Three-wheeled Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#three-wheeled-vehicles .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every three-wheeled vehicle shall be equipped with
    lamps, reflex reflectors and associated components as required under
    subsection (1) for passenger cars, and

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if an outermost parking lamp is installed more
      than 400 mm from the nearest of the two outer edges of the vehicle
      that are used to determine the overall width of the vehicle, with
      a white forward-facing reflex reflector that is installed not more
      than 400 mm from that edge to indicate the width of the vehicle;
      and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an outermost tail lamp or outermost rear
      reflex reflector is installed more than 400 mm from the nearest of
      the two outer edges of the vehicle that are used to determine the
      overall width of the vehicle, with a red rearward-facing reflex
      reflector that is installed not more than 400 mm from that edge to
      indicate the width of the vehicle.

  ##### [Alternative Lamps for Passenger Cars, Three-wheeled Vehicles, Multi-purpose Passenger Vehicles, Trucks and Buses]{.HTitleText3} {#alternative-lamps-for-passenger-cars-three-wheeled-vehicles-multi-purpose-passenger-vehicles-trucks-and-buses .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), instead of being
    equipped with headlamps as required under subsection (1) or (2), as
    the case may be, passenger cars, three-wheeled vehicles,
    multi-purpose passenger vehicles, trucks and buses may be equipped
    with adaptive driving beam lamps that conform to SAE Recommended
    Practice J3069, Adaptive Driving Beam (June 2016), except that the
    lamps shall not be capable of activation or deactivation by pedal.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the adaptive driving beam is formed by the
    entirety or a portion of an upper beam or lower beam, or both, the
    lamps shall also conform to TSD 108, except that despite S10.18,
    horizontal aim adjustment is allowed.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Instead of being equipped with headlamps as
    required under subsection (1) or (2), as the case may be, passenger
    cars, three-wheeled vehicles, multi-purpose passenger vehicles,
    trucks and buses may be equipped with headlamps that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a passenger car, multi-purpose
      passenger vehicle, truck or bus, conform to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 8, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Vehicle Headlamps
        Emitting an Asymmetrical Passing Beam or a Driving Beam or Both
        and Equipped with Halogen Filament Lamps (H~1~, H~2~, H~3~,
        HB~3~, HB~4~, H~7~, H~8~, H~9~, HIR1, HIR2 and/or H~11~), as
        amended from time to time,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 20, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Vehicle Headlamps
        Emitting an Asymmetrical Passing Beam or a Driving Beam or Both
        and Equipped with Halogen Filament Lamps (H~4~ Lamps), as
        amended from time to time,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 31, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Power-Driven Vehicle's
        Halogen Sealed-beam Headlamps (HSB) Emitting a European
        Asymmetrical Passing Beam or a Driving Beam or Both, as amended
        from time to time,

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 98, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Vehicle Headlamps
        Equipped with Gas-discharge Light Sources, as amended from time
        to time, or

      - [(v)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 112, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Vehicle Headlamps
        Emitting an Asymmetrical Passing-beam or a Driving-beam or Both
        and Equipped with Filament Lamps and/or Light-emitting Diode
        (LED) Modules, as amended from time to time;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a three-wheeled vehicle, conform
      to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the regulation referred to in subparagraph
        (a)(i), (ii) or (v), or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the regulation referred to in subparagraph
        (a)(iii) or (iv), as if the vehicle were a passenger car;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are installed on the vehicle in such a manner as
      to produce only a beam pattern for right-hand traffic, and, if the
      vehicle is fitted with a mechanism that allows the headlamps to
      produce a beam pattern for left-hand traffic, that mechanism is
      inoperative; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} conform to, as applicable, the physical tests
      referred to in the following provisions of TSD 108:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} S10.13.4,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} S10.14.7, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} S10.15.7.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} Instead of being equipped with headlamps in
    accordance with subsection (1) or (2), as the case may be, passenger
    cars, three-wheeled vehicles, multi-purpose passenger vehicles,
    trucks and buses may be equipped with headlamps that conform to
    United Nations Regulation No. 123, Uniform Provisions Concerning the
    Approval of Adaptive Front-lighting Systems (AFS) for Motor
    Vehicles, as amended from time to time.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} The headlamps referred to in subsection (6) shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be installed

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with paragraph 6.22 of United
        Nations Regulation No. 48, Uniform Provisions Concerning the
        Approval of Vehicles with Regard to the Installation of Lighting
        and Light-signalling Devices, as amended from time to time,
        except that, despite paragraph 6.22.6.2, the automatic levelling
        device is mandatory in all cases, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in such a manner as to produce only a beam
        pattern for right-hand traffic and, if the vehicle is fitted
        with a mechanism that allows the headlamps to produce a beam
        pattern for left-hand traffic, that mechanism shall be
        inoperative; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} above a mounting height of 850 mm, have a maximum
      intensity that does not exceed the intensity requirements under
      TSD 108 for upper beam headlamps where the mounting height is
      measured to the centre of the highest mounted lamp.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (5) and (6), the
    following requirements of the United Nations Regulations referred to
    in those subsections do not apply:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting the type-approval
      process;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting the marking of
      type-approved headlamps; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the conformity of production of type-approved
        headlamps,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the penalties for non-conformity of
        production, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the modification of a headlamp type and
        extension of approval.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6) and subparagraph
    (7)(a)(i), United Nations Regulations Nos. 48 and 123 are considered
    to be applicable to three-wheeled vehicles, despite any provision to
    the contrary in those Regulations.

  ##### [Motorcycles Other than Motor Tricycles]{.HTitleText3} {#motorcycles-other-than-motor-tricycles .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle other than a motor tricycle shall
    conform to TSD 108, except that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} S6.1.3.5.1 (Vertical headlamp arrangement) does
      not apply; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} despite S10.18, S10.18.1 applies to motorcycles
      except that "both vertical and horizontal aim" shall be read as
      "the vertical aim".

  ##### [Motor Tricycles]{.HTitleText3} {#motor-tricycles .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} Every motor tricycle shall conform to TSD 108,
    except that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following provisions of TSD 108 do not apply:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} S6.1.3.5.1 (Vertical headlamp arrangement), and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} S10.17.1.2.2 (distance between headlamps);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} despite S10.18, S10.18.1 applies to motor
      tricycles except that "both vertical and horizontal aim" shall be
      read as "the vertical aim";

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} despite S6.1.1 and S6.1.3.1 and Table I-c, a
      motor tricycle shall be equipped with tail lamps, stop lamps and
      reflex reflectors in the number and at the mounting locations
      specified in Table I-a for passenger cars; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} instead of being equipped with headlamps as
      specified in S6.1 and Table I-c, a motor tricycle may be equipped
      with lower beam headlamps and upper beam headlamps as specified in
      S6.1 and Table I-a for passenger cars.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} In addition to being equipped with reflex
    reflectors and lamps as specified in S6.1 and Table I-c of TSD 108,
    a motor tricycle shall be

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} equipped with parking lamps as specified in S6.1
      and Table I-a of TSD 108 for passenger cars;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an outermost parking lamp is installed more
      than 400 mm from the nearest of the two outer edges of the motor
      tricycle that are used to determine the overall width of the motor
      tricycle, equipped with a white forward-facing reflex reflector
      that is installed not more than 400 mm from that edge to indicate
      the width of the motor tricycle; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if an outermost tail lamp or outermost rear
      reflex reflector is installed more than 400 mm from the nearest of
      the two outer edges of the motor tricycle that are used to
      determine the overall width of the motor tricycle, equipped with a
      red rearward-facing reflex reflector that is installed not more
      than 400 mm from that edge to indicate the width of the motor
      tricycle.

  ##### [Alternative Headlamps for Motorcycles]{.HTitleText3} {#alternative-headlamps-for-motorcycles .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} Instead of being equipped with headlamps as
    required under subsection (10) or (11), as the case may be,
    motorcycles may be equipped with headlamps that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conform to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subsection (5),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 57, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Headlamps for Motor Cycles
        and Vehicles Treated as Such, as amended from time to time,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 72, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Cycle Headlamps
        Emitting an Asymmetrical Passing Beam and a Driving Beam and
        Equipped with Halogen Lamps (HS~1~ Lamps), as amended from time
        to time, or

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} United Nations Regulation No. 113, Uniform
        Provisions Concerning the Approval of Motor Vehicle Headlamps
        Emitting a Symmetrical Passing Beam or a Driving Beam or Both
        and Equipped with Filament, Gas-discharge Light Sources or LED
        Modules, as amended from time to time;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are installed in accordance with the requirements
      of paragraphs 5.7, 5.11, 5.13, 6.1 and 6.2 of United Nations
      Regulation No. 53, Uniform Provisions Concerning the Approval of
      Category L~3~ Vehicles with Regard to the Installation of Lighting
      and Light-Signalling Devices, as amended from time to time; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of headlamps conforming to a
      Regulation referred to in subparagraph (a)(ii), (iii) or (iv),
      conform to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the performance requirements of the vibration
        test, moisture test, dust test and corrosion test described in
        S14.5 of TSD 108, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of headlamps that do not
        incorporate a glass lens, the performance requirements of the
        plastic optical materials tests described in S14.4 of TSD 108.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (13), the following
    requirements of the United Nations Regulations referred to in that
    subsection do not apply:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting the type-approval
      process;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting the marking of
      type-approved headlamps; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any requirements respecting

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the conformity of production of type-approved
        headlamps,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the penalties for non-conformity of
        production, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the modification of a headlamp type and
        extension of approval.

  ##### [Restricted-use Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#restricted-use-vehicles .SchedHeadL3}

  ::: HistoricalNote
  \[
  - SOR/2020-22, s. 17

  \]
  :::

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} Every restricted-use vehicle shall be equipped
    with reflex reflectors as required under subsection (10) for
    motorcycles other than motor tricycles.

  ##### [Additional Requirements for the Activation of Certain Lamps]{.HTitleText3} {#additional-requirements-for-the-activation-of-certain-lamps .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} In addition to being activated as specified in
    Table I-a of TSD 108, parking lamps, tail lamps, licence plate lamps
    and side marker lamps on a passenger car, multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle, three-wheeled vehicle, truck or bus shall be activated

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} while the front fog lamps on the vehicle are
      activated in a steady-burning state other than as daytime running
      lamps; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} while the rear fog lamps on the vehicle are
      activated in a steady-burning state.

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} Except when it is used to give intermittent
    luminous warnings at short intervals, the upper beam may be
    activated only when the master light switch is in the "headlamps on"
    position or in the "AUTO" (automatic) position and the conditions
    for automatic activation of the lower beam exist.

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} Despite S6.1.5 and Table I-a of TSD 108, the tail
    lamps may be activated without the concurrent activation of the
    headlamps or parking lamps, if the daytime running lamps are
    activated.

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} Beginning on September 1, 2021, if the fuel level
    indicator, oil pressure indicator, engine coolant temperature
    indicator, battery charging indicator, transmission control position
    indicator or speedometer indicator or their identifications are
    illuminated when the daytime running lights of a vehicle are in use,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the lower beam headlamps shall

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} automatically activate in a steady-burning
        state in less than two seconds when the ambient light outside
        the vehicle is less than 1,000 lux while the transmission
        control is in the forward or reverse position and the vehicle is
        in motion, unless the lower beam headlamps were deactivated
        manually after their automatic activation following the most
        recent disarming of the immobilization system in accordance with
        section 114, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} automatically deactivate under the conditions
        set out by the manufacturer; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the tail lamps shall be alight.

  - [(20)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (19)(a), the ambient
    light outside a vehicle shall be measured on a horizontal surface,
    with a cosine corrected sensor at the same height as the mounting
    position of the ambient light sensor on the vehicle.

  ##### [Fog Lamps and Forward Auxiliary Road Illumination Lamps --- Aiming Adjustment Mechanism]{.HTitleText3} {#fog-lamps-and-forward-auxiliary-road-illumination-lamps-aiming-adjustment-mechanism .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(21)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle, three-wheeled vehicle, motor tricycle, truck and bus
    equipped with a front fog lamp or forward auxiliary road
    illumination lamp shall be equipped with a mechanism for that lamp
    that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} allows aim adjustment of the vertical aim by one
      person to maintain the beam pattern of the lamp within the full
      range of vertical pitch of the vehicle, with the use of ordinarily
      available tools; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allows the adjustment referred to in
      paragraph (a) after being subjected to the corrosion test
      procedure set out in S14.6.3.1 of TSD 108.

  ##### [Information]{.HTitleText3} {#information .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(22)]{.lawlabel} There shall be provided, in the owner's manual,
    with every passenger car, multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck and
    bus, the information required by TSD 108 in relation to the safe
    operation of the lighting systems and reflective devices of the
    vehicle.

  - [(23)]{.lawlabel} There shall be provided, in the owner's manual,
    with every three-wheeled vehicle, information relating to the safe
    operation of the lighting systems and reflective devices of the
    vehicle that is the same as the information required by TSD 108 in
    relation to passenger cars.

  - [(24)]{.lawlabel} Except for the words "sealed beam" referred to in
    S6.5.3.3.1 of TSD 108 and the word "motorcycle" referred to in
    S10.17.2 of TSD 108, any information required under this section to
    be marked on a passenger car, multi-purpose passenger vehicle,
    three-wheeled vehicle, motorcycle, restricted-use vehicle, trailer,
    truck or bus shall be in English and French.

  ##### [Daytime Running Lamps]{.HTitleText3} {#daytime-running-lamps .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(25)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (26) to (30) apply to passenger cars,
    multi-purpose passenger vehicles, trucks, buses and three-wheeled
    vehicles.

  - [(26)]{.lawlabel} Every vehicle shall be equipped with daytime
    running lamps

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with S6.1.3.1 of TSD 108, except
      that the reference to "applicable photometric requirements" in
      that provision is a reference to the applicable photometric
      requirements set out in SAE Standard J2087, Daytime Running Light
      (August 2017) (SAE Standard J2087);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with S6.2 of TSD 108, except that
      the references in that provision to "lighting equipment required
      by this TSD" and to "required lamp" include a reference to
      "daytime running lamp"; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that conform to

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} sections 5, 6 and 7.3.1 of SAE Standard J2087,
        except that despite section 6.1.2, if a daytime running lamp is
        optically combined with a white front position lamp that has a
        projected luminous lens area of 200 cm^2^ or less, it may
        conform to the light intensity distribution values shown in
        figure 1 when tested in accordance with section 5.1, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} until September 1, 2020, SAE Standard J583,
        Front Fog Lamps (September 2016) (SAE Standard J583).

  - [(27)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 6.4 of SAE Standard J2087, the
    light from a daytime running lamp shall be

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the lamp is optically combined with a parking
      lamp, white or yellow;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if produced by a turn signal lamp, yellow; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, white.

  ##### [Switching --- Daytime Running Lamps]{.HTitleText3} {#switching-daytime-running-lamps .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(28)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (29) and (30), the daytime
    running lamps on a vehicle shall be activated not later than when
    the vehicle is set in motion under its own power and shall remain
    activated until the motors or engines used for the vehicle's
    propulsion are switched off and as a result the vehicle enters a
    mode of operation other than "accessory" or "on".

  - [(29)]{.lawlabel} The daytime running lamps on a vehicle shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be deactivated while the lower beam headlamps are
      activated to provide road illumination;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} beginning on September 1, 2020, be deactivated
      while the front fog lamps provide road illumination after being
      activated by the driver; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if they also serve as front turn signal lamps,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be deactivated on the side of the vehicle where
        a turn signal is activated, while that turn signal is activated,
        and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} be deactivated on both sides of the vehicle
        while the hazard warning signal is activated.

  - [(30)]{.lawlabel} The daytime running lamps on a vehicle may be

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deactivated while the parking brake is applied or
      the transmission control is in the park or neutral position and
      the vehicle is not in motion;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} deactivated while the headlamps are being flashed
      for signalling purposes;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} switched off manually, in which case they shall
      reactivate automatically, at the discretion of the manufacturer,
      either not later than when the vehicle travels more than 100 m or
      when the speed of the vehicle exceeds 10 km/h; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} wired to flash for signalling purposes.

  ##### [Motorcycle Running Lamps]{.HTitleText3} {#motorcycle-running-lamps .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(31)]{.lawlabel} The following lamps on a motorcycle shall be
    activated not later than when the motorcycle is set in motion under
    its own power and shall remain activated until the motorcycle's main
    electrical system is turned off or until the motorcycle is put in
    the "accessory" mode of operation:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every tail lamp;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every licence plate lamp; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} every headlamp or, as an alternative, one or two
      lamps that conform to the requirements for dedicated daytime
      running lamps set out in SAE Standard J2087.

  ##### [TSD 108]{.HTitleText3} {#tsd-108 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(32)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, "equipment" in
    TSD 108 shall be read as "component".

  ##### [United Nations Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#united-nations-regulations .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(33)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} "dipped beam" and "passing beam" in United
      Nations Regulations Nos. 8, 20, 31, 48, 53, 57, 72, 98, 112, 113
      and 123 shall be read as "lower beam" or as "lower beam headlamp",
      as the context requires; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} "driving beam" and "main beam" in United Nations
      Regulations Nos. 8, 20, 31, 48, 53, 57, 72, 98, 112, 113 and 123
      shall be read as "upper beam" or as "upper beam headlamp", as the
      context requires.

  ##### [SAE]{.HTitleText3} {#sae .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(34)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 7.3.1 of SAE Standard
    J2087 and section 5.2.5.1 of SAE Standard J583, "should" shall be
    interpreted as expressing an obligation.

  #### [Transitional Provision]{.HTitleText2} {#transitional-provision-2 .SchedHeadL2}

  - [(35)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (34), a vehicle may,
    until August 31, 2019, conform to the requirements of this section
    that are applicable to a lighting system or reflective device, as
    they read immediately before the day on which this subsection comes
    into force.

- **108.1** [\[Repealed, SOR/2018-43, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Tire Selection and Rims for Motor Vehicles With a GVWR of 4 536 KG or Less (Standard 110)]{.HTitleText2} {#tire-selection-and-rims-for-motor-vehicles-with-a-gvwr-of-4-536-kg-or-less-standard-110 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#general-1 .SchedHeadL3}

- - **110** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every motor vehicle with a GVWR of 4 536 kg
    or less --- except motorcycles other than motor tricycles equipped
    with passenger car tires, restricted-use vehicles, three-wheeled
    vehicles equipped with tires other than passenger car tires and
    low-speed vehicles --- and every tire rim manufactured for use on
    those vehicles must conform to the requirements of Technical
    Standards Document No. 110, Tire Selection and Rims for Motor
    Vehicles With a GVWR of 4 536 kg or Less (TSD 110), as amended from
    time to time.

  ##### [Technical Standards Document No. 110]{.HTitleText3} {#technical-standards-document-no.-110 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in subsections (3) and (4), the
    information specified in S4.3 and S4.3.5 of TSD 110 shall appear, at
    the option of the manufacturer, either

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in both official languages on one vehicle
      placard, as shown in Figure 3, or, if the manufacturer chooses to
      use a tire inflation pressure label, on one placard and one label,
      as shown in Figures 3 and 6; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in each official language on two vehicle
      placards, as shown in Figures 1 and 2, or, if the manufacturer
      chooses to use a tire inflation pressure label, on two placards
      and two labels, as shown in Figures 1, 2, 4 and 5, affixed at the
      same location on the vehicle but apart.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The information specified in S4.3(f) of TSD 110
    that appears on a vehicle placard and, at the manufacturer's option,
    on a tire inflation pressure label, in accordance with paragraph
    (2)(a), shall appear either

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the form of the symbol number N.03 for
      Operator's manual, operation instructions, that is included in
      International Standard ISO 2575, entitled Road vehicles ---
      Symbols for controls, indicators and tell-tales, 7th edition, May
      1, 2004; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in both official languages on one placard, as
      shown in Figure 3, or on one placard and one label, as shown in
      Figures 3 and 6.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The information specified in S4.3(f) of TSD 110
    that appears on a vehicle placard and, at the manufacturer's option,
    on a tire inflation pressure label, in accordance with paragraph
    2(b), shall appear either

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the form of the symbol number N.03 specified
      in paragraph (3)(a); or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in each official language on two placards, as
      shown in Figures 1 and 2, or on two placards and two labels, as
      shown in Figures 1, 2, 4 and 5, affixed at the same location on
      the vehicle but apart.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} The words "[Voir le manuel de l'usager pour plus de
    renseignements]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", in the French version of the
    information specified in S4.3(f) of TSD 110, may, at the option of
    the manufacturer, be replaced by the words "[Voir le guide du
    propriétaire pour plus de renseignements]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "[Voir le manuel du propriétaire pour plus de
    renseignements]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or "[Voir le guide de
    l'automobiliste pour plus de renseignements]{.otherLang lang="fr"}".

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} The information specified in S4.3.3 of TSD 110
    shall be in both official languages.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply for the purposes of
    TSD 110.

    [[load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum load a
        tire is rated to carry at a given inflation pressure. ([charge
        nominale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[maximum load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[maximum load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the load
        rating at the maximum permissible inflation pressure for that
        tire. ([limite de charge nominale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[rim base]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[rim base]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means that portion of a rim
        remaining after the removal of all split or continuous rim
        flanges, side rings, and locking rings that can be detached from
        the rim. ([base de jante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  ##### [Load Range Identification Symbol]{.HTitleText3} {#load-range-identification-symbol .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} In the case of vehicles equipped with light-truck
    tires, the load range identification symbol shall appear either on
    the compliance label required by section 6 of these Regulations or
    on the vehicle placard or tire inflation pressure label, after the
    tire size designation.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Mirrors and Visibility Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#mirrors-and-visibility-systems .SchedHeadL2}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2024-239, s. 4

\]
:::

##### [Mirrors]{.HTitleText3} {#mirrors .SchedHeadL3}

###### [General]{.HTitleText4} {#general-2 .SchedHeadL4}

- - **111** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any mirror referred to in this section that
    is installed on a vehicle shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a stable support;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be adjustable in the horizontal and vertical
      directions;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be a unit magnification mirror, except in the
      cases referred to in subsection (6), paragraph (13)(b) and
      subsection (16);

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be free of sharp points or edges that could cause
      an injury to an occupant of the vehicle or to a pedestrian;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a System B mirror installed
      on a school bus that has a forward control configuration, be
      installed so that the driver's field of view through the mirror is
      not obscured by the portion of the windshield that is not wiped by
      the windshield wipers or by any opaque portion of the vehicle
      structure; and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an outside rearview mirror, have
      no greater protrusion beyond the perimeter of the vehicle than is
      necessary to meet the field-of-view requirements for the mirror
      prescribed in this section.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} A rearview mirror referred to in subsection (7)
    or (11) or paragraph (26)(a) shall be capable of adjustment from
    within the occupant compartment of the vehicle.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The average reflectance of a mirror referred to in
    this section shall be determined in accordance with SAE Standard
    J964, Test Procedure for Determining Reflectivity of Rear View
    Mirrors (June 1992).

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} A mirror referred to in this section that is a
    single reflectance mirror shall have a reflectance level of at least
    35 per cent.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} A mirror referred to in this section that is a
    multiple reflectance mirror shall have a daytime reflectance level
    of at least 35 per cent and a night-time reflectance level of at
    least 4 per cent. In the event of electrical failure, the mirror
    shall be adjustable, either manually or automatically, to a
    reflectance level of at least 35 per cent.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} An outside rearview mirror referred to in paragraph
    (7)(b) or subsection (26) or (27) that is installed on the side of
    the vehicle opposite the driver's side may be convex if

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its reflective surface area is equal to or
      greater than the reflective surface area that a unit magnification
      mirror must have in accordance with that paragraph or subsection;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its average radius of curvature is not less than
      890 mm (35 inches) and not greater than 1 800 mm (71.5 inches);
      and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the radius of curvature at any point does not
      deviate by more than 12.5 per cent from the average of any five
      radius-of-curvature measurements made on its reflective surface at
      least 6 mm (0.25 inch) from the edge of the image display.

  ###### [Passenger Cars and Three-wheeled Vehicles]{.HTitleText4} {#passenger-cars-and-three-wheeled-vehicles .SchedHeadL4}

  ::: HistoricalNote
  \[
  - SOR/2003-272, s. 13

  \]
  :::

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} An inside rearview mirror shall be installed on
    every passenger car and three-wheeled vehicle and shall, under the
    conditions prescribed in subsection (8),

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide the driver with a field of view to the
      rear that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is not less than 20° measured horizontally
        rearward from the projected eye point, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} extends to the horizon and includes a point on
        the road surface not more than 60 m (200 feet) directly behind
        the vehicle; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the inside rearview mirror does not provide
      the field of view to the rear described in paragraph (a), be
      accompanied, on the side opposite the driver's side, by an outside
      rearview mirror that has not less than 90 per cent of the
      reflective surface area of an outside rearview mirror installed
      pursuant to subsection (11).

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (7), the vehicle
    shall be on a level road surface and loaded with the lighter of the
    following loads, calculated on the basis of the driver and each
    occupant weighing 68 kg (150 pounds):

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a driver and four other occupants, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an occupant in each designated seating position.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} A field of view to the rear described in paragraph
    (7)(a) may be partially obscured by seated occupants or head
    restraints.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} An inside rearview mirror referred to in
    subsection (7), if situated in the head impact area, shall, when the
    reflective surface of the mirror is subjected to a force of 400 N
    (90 pounds) in any direction that is not more than 45° from the
    forward longitudinal direction, deflect, collapse or break away
    without leaving sharp edges.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} An outside rearview mirror shall be installed on
    the driver's side of every passenger car and three-wheeled vehicle
    in such a manner as to provide the driver with a field of view to
    the rear on a level road surface that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may be partially obscured by the rear body or
      fender contours;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} extends to the horizon; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} includes a line measuring 2.5 m (8 feet)
      perpendicular to and outboard from the vertical longitudinal plane
      tangent to the driver's side of the vehicle at its widest part, at
      a point 10.6 m (35 feet) behind the eyes of the driver seated with
      the driver's seat in the rearmost position.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (7), (9) and (11),
    the field of view to the rear of the driver shall be evaluated by
    using

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of the driver's eye reference points
      for the 95th percentile tangential cut-off specified in SAE
      Recommended Practice J941a, Passenger Car Driver's Eye Range
      (August 1967); or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the driver's eye reference points at a nominal
      location appropriate for any 95th percentile adult male driver.

  ###### [Motorcycles]{.HTitleText4} {#motorcycles .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle shall have, mounted on each side
    so that the horizontal centre of each reflective surface of the
    mirror is at least 280 mm (11 inches) outward from the longitudinal
    centreline of the motorcycle,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a rearview mirror with not less than 80 cm^2^
      (12.5 square inches) of reflective surface area; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a convex rearview mirror with not less than
      64.5 cm^2^ (10 square inches) of reflective surface area and an
      average radius of curvature that is not less than 510 mm (20
      inches) and not greater than 1 800 mm (71.5 inches).

  - [(13.1)]{.lawlabel} As an alternative to the rearview mirrors
    required by subsection (13), a motorcycle may be equipped with
    rearview mirrors that conform to paragraph 16 of United Nations
    Regulation No. 81, Uniform Provisions Concerning the Approval of
    Rear-view Mirrors of Two-wheeled Power-driven Vehicles with or
    without Side Car, with Regard to the Installation of Rear-view
    Mirrors on Handlebars (United Nations Regulation No. 81), as amended
    by any amendment prior to the 01 series of amendments.

  - [(13.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph 16.2.1 of United Nations
    Regulation No. 81, all two-wheeled vehicles with a maximum speed of
    50 km/h or less shall be equipped with two rearview mirrors.

  - [(13.3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (13.1) and
    (13.2), a reference in United Nations Regulation No. 81 to
    "two-wheeled vehicle" or "three-wheeled vehicle" is to be read as a
    reference to "motorcycle", and a reference to "maximum designed
    speed" is to be read as a reference to "maximum speed".

  ###### [School Buses]{.HTitleText4} {#school-buses .SchedHeadL4}

  ###### [General]{.HTitleText5} {#general-3}

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (16) to (25), a
    driver's eye position shall be represented by the left and right eye
    points as defined in SAE Recommended Practice J1050, Describing and
    Measuring the Driver's Field of View (August 1994), and shall be at
    any place within the area defined by a 95th percentile eyellipse in
    accordance with SAE Recommended Practice J941, Motor Vehicle
    Drivers' Eye Locations (June 1997), with the following adaptations:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a 50/50 male-to-female ratio must be used for the
      male/female mix; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [[heel point]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} referred to in
      that Recommended Practice and in other documents referenced in
      that Recommended Practice means the [[accelerator heel point
      (AHP)]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in section 3.16.1 of SAE
      Recommended Practice J1100, Motor Vehicle Dimensions (February
      2001), and the position of the heel point is that determined by
      the manufacturer.

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2008-72, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (16) to (25),

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cylinders A, D and E shall be 0.305 m (1 foot)
      high and 0.305 m (1 foot) in diameter;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cylinders B and C shall be 0.915 m (3 feet) high
      and 0.305 m (1 foot) in diameter; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cylinders A, B, C, D and E shall be of a colour
      that provides a high contrast with the road surface on which the
      bus is parked.

  ###### [Requirements]{.HTitleText5}

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} Every school bus shall have the following two
    outside mirror systems:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} System A, which consists, on each side of the
      school bus, of one unit magnification mirror that conforms to
      subsection (18) and one convex mirror that conforms to subsection
      (19); and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} System B, which consists, on each side of the
      school bus, of one convex mirror that conforms to subsections (20)
      to (24).

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} A System A mirror and System B mirror shall
    conform to the provisions referred to in subsection (16) at any
    driver's eye position, when they are adjusted in accordance with the
    manufacturer's instructions.

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} Each System A unit magnification mirror shall have
    a reflective surface area of not less than 325 cm^2^ (50 square
    inches) that provides, at the driver's eye position, a field of view
    that includes a continuous view rearward, of the side of the school
    bus and the road surface, which view shall begin no farther than
    60 m (200 feet) rearward of the mirror's surface and extend to the
    horizon when measured on a level road, as illustrated in Figure 1.

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} Each System A convex mirror shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide, at the driver's eye position, a field of
      view that includes continuous and complete views as illustrated in
      Figure 1,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} rearward,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} of the ground, which view overlaps the field
        of view provided by the unit magnification mirror described in
        subsection (18),

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} of the side of the bus,

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in the mirror installed on the side opposite
        the driver's side, of cylinders B and D, placed in accordance
        with subsection (25), and

      - [(v)]{.lawlabel} in the mirror installed on the driver's side,
        of cylinders C and E, placed in accordance with subsection (25);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have an average radius of curvature of not less
      than 482 mm (19 inches); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have a radius of curvature that does not deviate
      at any point by more than 12.5 per cent from the average of any
      five radius-of-curvature measurements taken at least 6 mm (0.25
      inch) from the edge of the reflective surface.

  - [(20)]{.lawlabel} Each System B mirror shall be installed so that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the distance from the driver's eye position to
      the centre of the mirror is at least 95.25 cm (37.5 inches); and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the slope of the mirror surface has no
      discontinuities.

  - [(21)]{.lawlabel} Each System B mirror shall provide a field of view
    that includes continuous and complete views at the driver's eye
    position, as illustrated in Figure 1, of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ground from the front bumper forward to a
      point where direct observation of the ground is possible;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ground and the side of the bus rearward of
      the front bumper, extending to and overlapping the field of view
      provided by the System A convex mirror;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a mirror installed on the side
      opposite the driver's side, of cylinders A and B, placed in
      accordance with subsection (25), and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a mirror installed on the driver's
      side, of cylinders A and C, placed in accordance with subsection
      (25).

  - [(22)]{.lawlabel} The images of cylinders A, B and C, placed in
    accordance with subsection (25), that are reflected in each System B
    mirror shall meet the following requirements:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the shortest angular width of the image shall be
      no less than 3 minutes of arc, measured using the following
      equation:

      ::: {#479504 .Paragraph}
      X/D ≥ 0,000873

      where

      [X]{.dfn}
      :   is the width of the image of the cylinder on the reflective
          surface,

      [D]{.dfn}
      :   is the distance between the centre point of the driver's eye
          position and the centre of the reflective surface, and

      [0.000873]{.dfn}
      :   is the tangent of 3 minutes of arc; and
      :::

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the shortest angular length of the image shall be
      no less than 9 minutes of arc, measured using the following
      equation:

      ::: {#479510 .Paragraph}
      Y/D ≥ 0,002618

      where

      [Y]{.dfn}
      :   is the length of the image of the cylinder on the reflective
          surface,

      [D]{.dfn}
      :   is the distance between the centre point of the driver's eye
          position and the centre of the reflective surface, and

      [0.002618]{.dfn}
      :   is the tangent of 9 minutes of arc.
      :::

  - [(23)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (22), a comparison
    chart, such as the one shown in Figure 2, may be used to measure the
    angular width and angular length of an image of a cylinder, where

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the comparison chart is placed in a vertical
      plane that contains the image to be evaluated;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plane of the comparison chart is
      perpendicular to the line of sight;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the image of the cylinder and the comparison
      chart are visible through the still or video camera's viewfinder;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a photograph is taken at the driver's eye
      position; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the image of the cylinder is larger than the
      references shown on the comparison chart.

  - [(24)]{.lawlabel} Images reflected in each System B mirror shall be
    located no less than 3 minutes of arc from the edge of the
    reflective surface, when measured at the driver's eye position.

  ###### [Testing]{.HTitleText5}

  - [(25)]{.lawlabel} A System A mirror and a System B mirror shall be
    tested as follows:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cylinders A, B, C, D and E shall be placed at the
      following locations, as illustrated in Figure 1, with measurements
      taken from the centre of the cylinder, as viewed from above:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} cylinder A shall be placed in front of the bus
        so that its centre passes through the bus's longitudinal
        centreline and its top is directly visible through the portion
        of the windshield wiped by the windshield wipers at the driver's
        eye position,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} cylinder B shall be placed on the side
        opposite the driver's side at a point where the cylinder is
        entirely visible through the convex mirrors of both System A and
        System B on that side so that its centre falls in a vertical
        plane that is 2 m (6.6 feet) to the right of, and perpendicular
        to, a vertical plane tangent to the bus's most outboard surface,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} cylinder C shall be placed on the driver's
        side at a point where the cylinder is entirely visible through
        the convex mirrors of both System A and System B on that side so
        that its centre falls in a vertical plane that is 2 m (6.6 feet)
        to the left of, and perpendicular to, a vertical plane tangent
        to the bus's most outboard surface,

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} cylinder D shall be placed on the side
        opposite the driver's side so that its centre falls in the
        vertical plane that passes through the centreline of the bus's
        rear-wheel axle and that is 2 m (6.6 feet) to the right of the
        bus's most outboard surface, and

      - [(v)]{.lawlabel} cylinder E shall be placed on the driver's side
        so that its centre falls in the vertical plane that passes
        through the centreline of the bus's rear-wheel axle and that is
        2 m (6.6 feet) to the left of the bus's most outboard surface;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every mirror shall be adjusted in accordance with
      the manufacturer's recommendations to the driver's eye position
      and is not to be moved or readjusted during testing for that eye
      position but may be readjusted for subsequent tests for different
      eye positions;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a still or video camera shall be positioned so
      that its image plane is located at the driver's eye point in such
      a manner that the reflective surface is visible to the camera
      through the windows of the bus;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for a specific driver's eye position, the
      requirements of subsections (16) to (25) shall be satisfied with
      the still or video camera positioned at either the left or right
      eye point;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the still or video camera shall be supported so
      as to allow pivoting

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the vertical and horizontal planes of its
        image plane to no greater than the maximum allowable limits of
        eye rotation specified in SAE Recommended Practice J1050,
        Describing and Measuring the Driver's Field of View (August
        1994), and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the horizontal plane of its image plane to
        no greater than the maximum allowable limit of neck rotation
        specified in SAE Recommended Practice J1050, at a point
        corresponding to the neck pivot point as specified in that
        Recommended Practice, only after the maximum limits of eye
        rotation have been reached;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} all of the still or video camera observations
      shall be done with the service door of the bus closed and all stop
      signal arms fully retracted; and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection (21), the front
      bumper shall be the forwardmost structural contour of the bumper
      excluding the fasteners, protruding discrete bumper stops, and any
      attached accessories such as crossing control arms, which shall be
      removed prior to testing.

  ###### [Other Vehicles]{.HTitleText4} {#other-vehicles .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(26)]{.lawlabel} Every multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck and
    bus, with a GVWR of 4 536 kg (10,000 pounds) or less, other than a
    school bus, shall have

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following rearview mirrors, namely,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an inside rearview mirror that meets the
        requirements of subsections (7), (9) and (10),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on the driver's side, an outside rearview
        mirror that meets the field-of-view requirements of subsection
        (11), and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on the side opposite the driver's side, an
        outside rearview mirror that has not less than 90 per cent of
        the reflective surface area of the outside rearview mirror
        installed on the driver's side; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on each side of the vehicle, an outside rearview
      mirror of which not less than 125 cm^2^ (19.5 square inches) of
      reflective surface area is located so as to provide the driver
      with a view to the rear along both sides of the vehicle.

  - [(27)]{.lawlabel} Every multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck and
    bus with a GVWR of more than 4 536 kg (10,000 pounds), other than a
    school bus, shall have on each side of the vehicle an outside
    rearview mirror of which not less than 325 cm^2^ (50 square inches)
    of reflective surface area is located so as to provide the driver
    with a view to the rear along both sides of the vehicle.

  ###### [Shipment]{.HTitleText4} {#shipment .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(28)]{.lawlabel} A company may ship a vehicle bearing a compliance
    label or information label, as the case may be, on which no outside
    mirrors have been installed, if the applicable outside mirrors and
    all of the hardware that is necessary for their mounting accompany
    the vehicle and all of the holes that are necessary for mounting
    those mirrors have been made in the sheet metal of the vehicle.

  ##### [Visibility Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#visibility-systems .SchedHeadL3}

  ###### [Rear Visibility Systems]{.HTitleText4} {#rear-visibility-systems .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(29)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (32), every passenger car
    and three-wheeled vehicle with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less that is
    manufactured on or after the day specified in S5.5(b) of section
    571.111, chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of Federal Regulations of
    the United States, as amended from time to time, shall be equipped
    with a rear visibility system that conforms to the requirements for
    rear visibility set out in S5.5, other than S5.5(a), of that
    section, as amended from time to time, and that is tested in
    accordance with the rear visibility test procedure set out in S14 of
    that section, as amended from time to time.

  - [(30)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (33), every multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle, low-speed vehicle, truck and bus with a GVWR of 4
    536 kg or less that is manufactured on or after the day specified in
    S6.2(b) of section 571.111, chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of
    Federal Regulations of the United States, as amended from time to
    time, shall be equipped with a rear visibility system that conforms
    to the requirements for rear visibility set out in S6.2, other than
    S6.2(a), of that section, as amended from time to time, and that is
    tested in accordance with the rear visibility test procedure set out
    in S14 of that section, as amended from time to time.

  - [(31)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (29) and (30),

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [[rear visibility system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
      has the same meaning as in S4 of section 571.111, chapter V, Title
      49 of the Code of Federal Regulations of the United States, as
      amended from time to time;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to the term "backing event",
      "environmental test fixture", "external component", "key",
      "rearview image" or "rear visibility system" in S4, S5.5, S6.2 or
      S14 of section 571.111, chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of Federal
      Regulations of the United States, as amended from time to time, is
      to be read as a reference to that term as defined in S4; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference to "starting system" in S4, including
      its definition, or S14 of section 571.111, chapter V, Title 49 of
      the Code of Federal Regulations of the United States, as amended
      from time to time, is to be read as a reference to "ignition
      switch".

  - [(32)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (29) does not apply to a vehicle to
    which an information label has been applied under subsection 6.4(1),
    to which a compliance label has been applied under paragraph
    6.6(1)(b) or to which an additional label has been applied under
    paragraph 9(1)(c) if the vehicle is manufactured before the first
    anniversary of the day specified in S5.5(b) of section 571.111,
    chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of Federal Regulations of the United
    States, as amended from time to time.

  - [(33)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (30) does not apply to a vehicle to
    which an information label has been applied under subsection 6.4(1),
    to which a compliance label has been applied under paragraph
    6.6(1)(b) or to which an additional label has been applied under
    paragraph 9(1)(c) if the vehicle is manufactured before the first
    anniversary of the day specified in S6.2(b) of section 571.111,
    chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of Federal Regulations of the United
    States, as amended from time to time.

  ###### [Perimeter Visibility Systems]{.HTitleText4} {#perimeter-visibility-systems .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(34)]{.lawlabel} Every school bus other than a multifunction school
    activity bus must be equipped with a perimeter visibility system
    that conforms to the requirements set out in Document No. 111 ---
    Perimeter Visibility Systems (Document 111), as amended from time to
    time.

  - [(35)]{.lawlabel} The perimeter visibility system must include a
    device that displays to the driver in real time at most two images
    of the area that is immediately beyond the perimeter of the school
    bus and that would be, without the system, difficult or impossible
    for the driver to see. The boundaries of the area must be determined
    in accordance with Document 111.

  - [(36)]{.lawlabel} The device must be securely mounted in the
    driver's forward field of view and must not obstruct the driver's
    view of the road or of any controls or displays.

  - [(37)]{.lawlabel} The device must automatically display the images
    and must only do so when the school bus's propulsion system is
    activated and the school bus is fully stopped or travelling at a
    speed less than 15 km/h. However, the device may display the images
    when the school bus's propulsion system is deactivated.

  ###### [Transitional Provision]{.HTitleText4} {#transitional-provision-3 .SchedHeadL4}

  - [(38)]{.lawlabel} Until November 1, 2027, a school bus referred to
    in subsection (34) may conform to the requirements of this section
    as it read on the day before the day on which this subsection comes
    into force.

  - []{.lawlabel}

#### [Hood Latch System]{.HTitleText2} {#hood-latch-system .SchedHeadL2}

- - **113** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
    [[hood]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any exterior movable body panel
    forward of the windshield that is used to cover an engine, luggage,
    storage or battery compartment.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each hood with which a vehicle is equipped shall be
    provided with a hood latch system.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} A front-opening hood that, in any position,
    partially or completely obstructs a driver's forward view through
    the windshield of the vehicle shall be provided with a second
    latching position on the hood latch system or with a second hood
    latch system.

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/79-306, s. 4
  - SOR/79-940, s. 7
  - SOR/2000-182, s. 6
  :::

#### [Theft Protection and Rollaway Prevention (Standard 114)]{.HTitleText2} {#theft-protection-and-rollaway-prevention-standard-114 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **114** [(1)]{.lawlabel} With the exception of a walk-in van, every
    passenger car, every three-wheeled vehicle, and every multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle and truck with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less shall
    conform to the requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 114,
    Theft Protection and Rollaway Prevention (TSD 114), as amended from
    time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The term [[self-mobility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
    TSD 114 means movement of a vehicle under its own power.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

  ##### [Immobilization System]{.HTitleText3} {#immobilization-system .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} With the exception of a walk-in van and an
    emergency vehicle, every passenger car, every three-wheeled vehicle,
    and every multi-purpose passenger vehicle and truck with a GVWR of
    4 536 kg or less shall be equipped with an immobilization system
    that conforms to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one of the following sets of requirements as
      modified by subsection (22), (23) or (24):

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of section 3, subsection 4.3,
        sections 6 to 10 and subsections 12.1, 12.2 and 12.16 of
        National Standard of Canada CAN/ULC-S338-98, entitled Automobile
        Theft Deterrent Equipment and Systems: Electronic Immobilization
        (May 1998), published by the Underwriters' Laboratories of
        Canada,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the general and particular specifications that
        are set out in Part III of United Nations Regulation No. 97,
        entitled Uniform Provisions Concerning the Approval of Vehicle
        Alarm Systems (VAS) and of Motor Vehicles with Regard to Their
        Alarm Systems (AS), in the version in effect on August 8, 2007,
        or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the general and particular specifications
        that are set out in Part IV of United Nations Regulation No.
        116, entitled Uniform Provisions Concerning the Protection of
        Motor Vehicles Against Unauthorized Use, in the version in
        effect on February 10, 2009; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the requirements set out in subsections (8) to
      (21).

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} A vehicle equipped with an immobilization system
    shall be accompanied by the following written information:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} instructions for operating and maintaining the
      system; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a warning not to leave a disarming device or a
      combination that disarms the system in the vehicle.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} The information shall be provided in English,
    French or both official languages, as requested by the first retail
    purchaser of the vehicle.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[disarming
    device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a physical device that contains
    or transmits the code that disarms the immobilization system of a
    vehicle.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (9), an immobilization system
    shall arm automatically within a period of not more than 1 minute
    after the disarming device is removed from the vehicle, if the
    vehicle remains in a mode of operation other than "accessory" or
    "on" throughout that period.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the disarming device is a keypad or biometric
    identifier, the immobilization system shall arm automatically within
    a period of not more than 1 minute after the motors used for the
    vehicle's propulsion are turned off, if the vehicle remains in a
    mode of operation other than "accessory" or "on" throughout that
    period.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} The immobilization system shall arm automatically
    not later than 2 minutes after the immobilization system is
    disarmed, unless

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} action is taken for starting one or more motors
      used for the vehicle's propulsion;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disarming requires an action to be taken on

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the engine start control or electric motor
        start control,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the engine stop control or electric motor stop
        control, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the ignition switch; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} disarming occurs automatically by the presence of
      a disarming device and the device is inside the vehicle.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} If armed, the immobilization system

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall prevent the vehicle from moving more than
      3 m under its own power by inhibiting the operation of at least
      one electronic control unit; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall not have any impact on the vehicle's brake
      system except that it may prevent regenerative braking and the
      release of the parking brake.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} During the disarming process, a code shall be sent
    to the inhibited electronic control unit in order to allow the
    vehicle to move under its own power.

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} It shall not be possible to disarm the
    immobilization system by interrupting its normal operating voltage.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} When the normal starting procedure requires that
    the disarming device mechanically latch into a receptacle and the
    device is physically separate from the ignition switch key, one or
    more motors used for the vehicle's propulsion shall start only after
    the device is removed from that receptacle.

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} The immobilization system shall conform to the
    following requirements:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it shall have a minimum capacity of 50,000 code
      variants;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it shall not be disarmed by a code that can
      disarm all other immobilization systems of the same make and
      model; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (16), it shall not have the
      capacity to process more than 5,000 codes within 24 hours;

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} If an immobilization system uses rolling or
    encrypted codes, it may conform to the following requirements,
    instead of the requirement set out in paragraph (15)(c):

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the probability of obtaining the correct code
      within 24 hours shall not exceed 4 per cent; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it shall not be possible to disarm the system by
      re-transmitting in any sequence the previous 5 codes generated by
      the system.

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} The immobilization system shall be designed so
    that, when tested as installed in the vehicle,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} neither the replacement of an original
      immobilization system component with a manufacturer's replacement
      component nor the addition of a manufacturer's component can be
      completed without the use of software; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is not possible for the vehicle to move under
      its own power for at least 5 minutes after the beginning of the
      replacement or addition of a component referred to in paragraph
      (a).

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} The immobilization system's conformity to
    subsection (17) shall be demonstrated by testing that is carried out
    without damaging the vehicle.

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (17)(b) does not apply to the addition
    of a disarming device that requires the use of another disarming
    device that is validated by the immobilization system.

  - [(20)]{.lawlabel} The immobilization system shall be designed so
    that it can neither be bypassed nor rendered ineffective in a manner
    that would allow a vehicle to move under its own power, or be
    disarmed, using one or more of the tools and equipment listed in
    subsection (21),

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within a period of less than 5 minutes, when
      tested as installed in the vehicle; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within a period of less than 2.5 minutes, when
      bench-tested outside the vehicle.

  - [(21)]{.lawlabel} During a test referred to in subsection (20), only
    the following tools or equipment may be used:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} scissors, wire strippers, wire cutters and
      electrical wires;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hammer, a slide hammer, a chisel, a punch, a
      wrench, a screwdriver and pliers;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} steel rods and spikes;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a hacksaw;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a battery operated drill;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a battery operated angle grinder; and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a battery operated jigsaw.

  - [(22)]{.lawlabel} The requirements set out in National Standard of
    Canada CAN/ULC-S338-98 are modified as follows:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements respecting non-OEM systems as
      defined in section 2 of that document and respecting local noise
      regulations do not apply;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to a "manufacturer of the electronic
      immobilization system" is to be read as a reference to a
      "manufacturer"; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 8.1 is to be read as follows:
      "Transponders and remote controls shall be in sealed enclosures
      that conform to the requirements of Subsections 12.1, General, and
      12.2, Normal Operation."

  - [(23)]{.lawlabel} The requirements set out in Part III of United
    Nations Regulation No. 97 are modified as follows:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the aftermarket, type approval and radio
      transmission requirements do not apply;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all references to paragraph 33 do not apply;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of paragraph 31.7, the word
      [[rapidly]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means less than 5 minutes and the
      words [[time consuming]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} mean at least 5
      minutes; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} despite any statement to the contrary in
      paragraphs 31.10 and 31.11, the immobilization system shall not
      have any impact on the vehicle's brake system.

  - [(24)]{.lawlabel} The requirements set out in Part IV of United
    Nations Regulation No. 116 are modified as follows:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the aftermarket, type approval and radio
      transmission requirements do not apply;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of paragraph 8.2.7, the word
      [[rapidly]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means less than 5 minutes and the
      expression [[time consuming]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means at least 5
      minutes;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} despite any statement to the contrary in
      paragraphs 8.2.10 and 8.2.11, the immobilization system shall not
      have any impact on the vehicle's brake system; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all references to paragraph 8.4 do not apply.

#### [Vehicle Identification Number]{.HTitleText2} {#vehicle-identification-number .SchedHeadL2}

- - **115** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every vehicle shall have a vehicle
    identification number and that vehicle identification number shall
    not be the same as the vehicle identification number of a vehicle
    having a model year of 1980 or later manufactured within the
    preceding 60 years.

  - [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A vehicle that is manufactured from an incomplete
    vehicle shall bear the vehicle identification number assigned by the
    incomplete vehicle manufacturer.

  - [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Where a vehicle identification number is stated
    on a label bearing a statement of compliance affixed to a vehicle
    and the vehicle is altered, the vehicle identification number shall
    apply to the altered vehicle.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2.1), the vehicle
    identification number of each vehicle shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be composed of capital, sanserif characters;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be sunk into, embossed on or imprinted, clearly
      and indelibly, and in such a manner that the vehicle
      identification number cannot be removed without damaging or
      defacing the plate, label or vehicle, on

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an integral or structural part of the vehicle
        other than the dash-board pad or dash-board cover,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the dash-board or dash-board cover if it is
        not designed to be replaced following an air bag deployment, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a separate plate or label that is permanently
        affixed in a location referred to in subparagraph (i) or (ii);
        and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a low-speed vehicle, multi-purpose
      passenger vehicle, passenger car, three-wheeled vehicle or truck
      having a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less, be

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} composed of characters having a minimum height
        of 4 mm,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} located inside the occupant compartment, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} without any part of such vehicle being
        removed, readable through the vehicle glazing under daylight
        conditions by an observer having 20/20 vision Snellen whose
        eyepoint is located outside the vehicle adjacent to the left
        windshield pillar.

  - [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The vehicle identification number of a snowmobile
    shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be composed of capital, sans serif characters;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be sunk into, embossed on, imprinted on or
      permanently affixed on the right exterior vertical surface of the
      track tunnel;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be difficult to remove, replace or alter without
      detection;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be composed of characters having a minimum height
      of 4 mm;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be legible and indelible;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} be legible without any part of the vehicle having
      to be removed; and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} be protected from corrosion.

  - [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} At the option of the manufacturer, the vehicle
    identification number may also be displayed in a bar code format
    that shall meet the requirements of section 5.6 of guideline AIAG
    B-10, Trading Partner Labels Implementation Guideline (February,
    2000), except for the specifications respecting code density and
    dimensions, which shall meet the requirements of section 4.1 of
    standard AIAG B-2, Vehicle Identification Number Label Standard
    (June, 1988).

  - [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} Every bar coded vehicle identification number
    shall be displayed on

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle's compliance or information label; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a separate label that is applied beside the
      compliance or information label in accordance with paragraphs 7(a)
      and (b) of these Regulations.

  - [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} If the vehicle identification number is displayed
    on a label in a bar code format, it need not, until September 1,
    2012, also be applied to the vehicle in accordance with paragraph
    115(2)(b), but may, at the option of the manufacturer, be sunk into,
    embossed on or imprinted, clearly and indelibly, on

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any part of the vehicle that is not designed to
      be removed except for repair, other than glazing; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a separate plate or label that is permanently
      affixed to a part referred to in paragraph (a).

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vehicle identification number of any vehicle
    shall be alphanumeric and shall be composed of 17 characters as
    follows:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the first three characters, the third of which
      shall not be "9", shall uniquely identify the manufacturer and the
      prescribed class of vehicle, if the manufacturer manufactures
      1,000 or more vehicles of a prescribed class annually;

    - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the first three characters, the third of which
      shall be "9", and the twelfth to fourteenth characters shall
      uniquely identify the manufacturer and the prescribed class of
      vehicle, if the manufacturer manufactures less than 1,000 vehicles
      of a prescribed class annually;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fourth to eighth characters, inclusive, shall
      uniquely identify the decipherable information for the vehicle as
      set out in Column II of Table I, except that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the seventh character shall be alphabetic for a
        multi-purpose passenger vehicle, passenger car, three-wheeled
        vehicle or truck having a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} subject to subparagraph (i), the characters
        and their positioning may be determined by the manufacturer, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an incomplete vehicle to be
        completed as a trailer, the decipherable information required
        for an incomplete vehicle in column II of Table I shall be that
        for a trailer;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the ninth character shall be the check digit
      determined in accordance with subsections (7) and (8) after all
      other characters have been determined by the manufacturer;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the tenth character shall be the code that
      corresponds to the vehicle model year as set out in Table II;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the eleventh character shall identify the plant
      of manufacture of the vehicle;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the twelfth to seventeenth characters shall be
      sequentially assigned by the manufacturer during the manufacturing
      process, if the manufacturer manufactures 1,000 or more vehicles
      of a prescribed class annually;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the fifteenth to seventeenth characters shall be
      sequentially assigned by the manufacturer during the manufacturing
      process, if the manufacturer manufactures less than 1,000 vehicles
      of a prescribed class annually; and

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the fourteenth to seventeenth characters shall be
      numeric for all vehicles and the thirteenth character shall be
      numeric if the vehicle is a multi-purpose passenger vehicle,
      passenger car, three-wheeled vehicle or truck having a GVWR of
      4 536 kg or less.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2004-250, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Each character used in a vehicle identification
    number shall be one of the Arabic numerals or Roman letters
    specified in Table III.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/88-535, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} The check digit referred to in paragraph (3)(c)
    shall be determined by

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assigning to each numeral of the vehicle
      identification number the actual mathematical value of that
      numeral;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} assigning to each letter the mathematical value
      for that letter specified in Table IV;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} multiplying the assigned value for each character
      of the vehicle identification number by the weight factor set out
      opposite that character in Table V; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} adding together the numbers obtained under
      paragraph (c) and dividing the total by 11.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} The fractional portion of the quotient obtained by
    paragraph (7)(d), multiplied by 11, shall be the check digit, except
    that if that product is 10, the check digit shall be X.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may require manufacturers of vehicles
    in respect of which this section applies to submit to the Minister,
    for each make and prescribed class of vehicle manufactured, those
    characters that uniquely identify the vehicle, including, where
    applicable, the twelfth to fourteenth characters, inclusive, that
    constitute part of the identifier, and, in all cases, the
    information necessary to decipher the characters in vehicle
    identification numbers.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} If a character in a vehicle identification number
    identifies engine net power, the engine net power represented by
    that character shall not differ by more than 10 per cent from the
    actual engine net power.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} Manufacturers of vehicles in respect of which this
    section applies shall apply to the Canadian Vehicle Manufacturers'
    Association for the characters referred to in paragraphs 3(a) and
    (a.1) that uniquely identify the manufacturer and prescribed class
    of vehicle.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (11), every vehicle
    having a model year of 2009 or earlier shall comply with the
    requirements of this section as it read on the day before the day on
    which this subsection came into force.

  - []{.lawlabel}
