    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Prescribed
    Class of Vehicle</th>
    <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Decipherable
    Information</th>
    </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Passenger car or
    three-wheeled vehicle</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, line, series, body type, engine type
    and all restraint system types and their location in the vehicle</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, line, series, body type, engine type
    and gross vehicle weight rating. In addition, for multipurpose passenger
    vehicles with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less, all restraint system types and
    their location in the vehicle.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Truck</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, model or line, series, chassis, cab
    type, engine type, brake system and gross vehicle weight rating. In
    addition, for trucks with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less, all restraint
    system types and their location in the vehicle.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">4</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Bus</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, model or line, series, body type,
    engine type and brake system</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">5</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Trailer</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, type of trailer, body type, length and
    axle configuration</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">6</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Motorcycle or restricted-use
    vehicle</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, type of cycle, line, engine type and
    engine net power</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">7</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Incomplete vehicle</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, model or line, series, cab type, engine
    type and brake system</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">8</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Trailer converter dolly</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, series and axle configuration</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">9</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Snowmobile</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">Make, type of snowmobile, line, engine type
    and engine net power</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight">10</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight">Low-speed vehicle</td>
    <td>Make, engine type, brake system, all restraint system types, body
    type and gross vehicle weight rating</td>
    </tr>
    </tbody>
    </table>
    <figcaption><p>TABLE I</p></figcaption>
    </figure>

#### [Motor Vehicle Brake Fluids (Standard 116)]{.HTitleText2} {#motor-vehicle-brake-fluids-standard-116 .SchedHeadL2}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/93-561, s. 5
- SOR/97-421, s. 7(F)
- SOR/2011-238, s. 2

\]
:::

- - **116** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The hydraulic brake system of a every
    passenger car, multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck, bus,
    three-wheeled vehicle, motorcycle, trailer, and trailer converter
    dolly shall contain brake fluid that conforms to the requirements of
    Technical Standards Document No. 116, Motor Vehicle Brake Fluids
    (TSD 116), as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} A reference in TSD 116 to a standard published by
    ASTM that is set out in column 1 of the table to this subsection may
    be read as a reference to the standard set out opposite that
    standard in column 2 of the table.

    <figure id="479741">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize7 topdouble topbot">
    <thead>
    <tr class="topdouble">
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column 1</th>
    <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column 2</th>
    </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">ASTM D 445 – 65, Viscosity of
    Transparent and Opaque Liquids (Kinematic and Dynamic Viscosities)</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">ASTM D 445 – 06, Standard Test Method for
    Kinematic Viscosity of Transparent and Opaque Liquids (and Calculation
    of Dynamic Viscosity)</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1121 – 67, Standard
    Method of Test for Reserve Alkalinity of Engine Antifreezes and
    Antirusts</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1287 – 91 (reapproved in 2002),
    Standard Test Method for pH of Engine Coolants and Antirusts</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1123 – 59, Standard
    Method of Test for Water in Concentrated Engine Antifreezes by the
    Iodine Reagent Method</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1123 – 99 (reapproved in 2003),
    Standard Test Methods for Water in Engine Coolant Concentrate by the
    Karl Fischer Reagent Method</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">4</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1193 – 70, Standard
    Specifications for Reagent Water</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">ASTM D 1193 – 06, Standard Specification for
    Reagent Water</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">5</td>

#### [Power-Operated Window, Partition and Roof Panel Systems (Standard 118)]{.HTitleText2} {#power-operated-window-partition-and-roof-panel-systems-standard-118 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **118** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every power-operated window system,
    power-operated partition system and power-operated roof panel system
    fitted on a passenger car, a three-wheeled vehicle, an enclosed
    motorcycle or on a multi-purpose passenger vehicle or a truck with a
    GVWR of 4 536 kg or less shall conform to the requirements of
    Technical Standards Document No. 118, Power-Operated Window,
    Partition, and Roof Panel Systems (TSD 118), as amended from time to
    time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, vehicles manufactured before October 1,
    2010, need not comply with the requirements of S6 of TSD 118.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

  ::: HistoricalNote

  - SOR/2003-272, s. 16
  - SOR/2006-94, s. 4(E)
  - SOR/2007-180, s. 8
  - SOR/2011-238, s. 2
  - SOR/2014-307, s. 6
  :::

#### [Tire Selection and Rims for Motor Vehicles With a GVWR of More Than 4 536 KG (Standard 120)]{.HTitleText2} {#tire-selection-and-rims-for-motor-vehicles-with-a-gvwr-of-more-than-4-536-kg-standard-120 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#general-4 .SchedHeadL3}

- - **120** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every motor vehicle with a GVWR of more
    than 4 536 kg, every three-wheeled vehicle equipped with tires other
    than passenger car tires, every motorcycle other than motor
    tricycles equipped with passenger car tires and every tire rim
    manufactured for use on those vehicles must conform to the
    requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 120, Tire Selection
    and Rims for Motor Vehicles With a GVWR of More Than 4 536 kg (TSD
    120), as amended from time to time.

  ##### [Technical Standards Document No. 120]{.HTitleText3} {#technical-standards-document-no.-120 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to conforming to the requirements of
    S5.1.3 of TSD 120, used or retreaded tires installed on a bus,
    trailer, trailer converter dolly or truck shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not have been the subject of a notice of defect;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a tread depth greater than 1.5 mm; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have been originally manufactured to conform to
      the requirements of any of the following that applied at the time
      of manufacture:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of used tires, the [Motor Vehicle
        Tire Safety Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2013-198) or the
        [Motor Vehicle Tire Safety Regulations,
        1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-95-148), and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of retreaded tires, section 4 of
        the [Motor Vehicle Tire Safety
        Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2013-198), Schedule V to the
        [Motor Vehicle Tire Safety Regulations,
        1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-95-148), the United States Federal
        Motor Vehicle Safety Standard No. 119 or the Japanese Industrial
        Standard JIS D4230.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The information specified in S5.3 of TSD 120 shall
    be in both official languages.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply for the purposes of
    TSD 120.

    [[load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum load a
        tire is rated to carry at a given inflation pressure. ([charge
        nominale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[maximum load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[maximum load rating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the load
        rating at the maximum permissible inflation pressure for that
        tire. ([limite de charge nominale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  ##### [Load Range Identification Symbol]{.HTitleText3} {#load-range-identification-symbol-1 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the case of vehicles equipped with light-truck
    tires, the load range identification symbol shall appear either on
    the compliance label required by section 6 of these Regulations or
    on the tire information label.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Air Brake Systems (Standard 121)]{.HTitleText2} {#air-brake-systems-standard-121 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **121** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), this section
    applies in respect of every truck, bus and trailer that is equipped
    with an air brake system.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of the
    following trucks, buses and trailers:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a trailer that has a width of more than 2.6 m
      with extendable equipment in the fully retracted position and that
      is equipped with two short-track axles in a line across the width
      of the trailer;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a truck, bus or trailer that is equipped with an
      axle that has a gross axle weight rating of 13 154 kg or more;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a truck or bus that has a speed attainable in
      3.2 km (2 miles) of not more than 53.1 km/h;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a truck that has a speed attainable in 3.2 km (2
      miles) of not more than 72.3 km/h, an unloaded vehicle mass of not
      less than 95% of its GVWR, and no capacity to carry occupants
      other than the driver and the operating crew;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a trailer that has a GVWR of more than 54 432 kg
      and whose body conforms to that described in the definition
      [[heavy hauler trailer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1)
      of these Regulations;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a trailer that has an unloaded vehicle mass of
      not less than 95% of its GVWR; and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a load divider dolly.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every truck, bus and trailer shall conform to the
    requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 121, Air Brake
    Systems (TSD 121), as amended from time to time.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a truck or bus is equipped with a front brake
    pressure limiting valve, that valve shall be automatic and shall
    operate while the service brakes are applied.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any antilock brake system malfunction indicator
    referred to in S5.1.6.2 of TSD 121 shall be the antilock brake
    system tell-tale set out in section 101.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} The parking brake static retardation force test
    that is referred to in S5.6.1 of TSD 121 shall be conducted in both
    a forward and a rearward direction.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Motorcycle Brake Systems (Standard 122)]{.HTitleText2} {#motorcycle-brake-systems-standard-122 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#general-5 .SchedHeadL3}

- - **122** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle shall conform to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of Technical Standards Document
      No. 122, Motorcycle Brake Systems (TSD 122), as amended from time
      to time; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the requirements set out in paragraphs 5 and 6
      and Annex 3 of United Nations Regulation No. 78, Revision 1,
      Uniform Provisions Concerning the Approval of Vehicles of
      Categories L~1~, L~2~, L~3~, L~4~ and L~5~ with Regard to Braking,
      in the version in effect on June 24, 2008, as amended by any
      subsequent amendments in the 03 series of amendments (United
      Nations Regulation No. 78).

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a motorcycle is designed to operate with or
    without a sidecar, it shall conform to the requirements of
    subsection (1) in each of those configurations.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle shall have a mark containing the
    symbol "DOT" followed by a reference to the type of brake fluid
    recommended by the manufacturer.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The mark shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be permanently affixed and of a colour that
      contrasts with its background, or be engraved or embossed;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be located, so as to be visible without
      obstruction, either on or within 101.6 mm of the brake-fluid
      reservoir filler plug or cap; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have letters and numbers at least 2.38 mm in
      height.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} The English and French versions of the owner's
    manual shall include, respectively, the English or French version of
    the following warning about brake fluid:

    "WARNING: Clean filler cap before removing. Use only \[here insert
    the information contained on the mark referred to in subsection
    (3)\] fluid from a sealed container."

    [« AVERTISSEMENT : Nettoyer le bouchon de remplissage avant de
    l'enlever. Utiliser seulement du liquide \[insérer ici les
    renseignements figurant sur la marque visée au paragraphe (3)\]
    provenant d'un contenant scellé. »]{.otherLang lang="fr"}

  ##### [Technical Standards Document No. 122]{.HTitleText3} {#technical-standards-document-no.-122 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of TSD 122, the definitions
    [[antilock brake system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or
    [[ABS]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[control]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
    [[split service brake system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[stopping
    distance]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1) of these
    Regulations, and section 5.3 of these Regulations, do not apply.

  - [(7) to (11)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2015-111, s.
    2\]]{.Repealed}

  ##### [United Nations Regulation No. 78]{.HTitleText3} {#united-nations-regulation-no.-78 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference to vehicle category "L~1~" in United
      Nations Regulation No. 78 is a reference to a limited-speed
      motorcycle that is designed to travel on two wheels in contact
      with the ground and that has a maximum speed of 50 km/h or less;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to vehicle category "L~2~" in United
      Nations Regulation No. 78 is a reference to a limited-speed
      motorcycle that is designed to travel on three wheels in contact
      with the ground and that has a maximum speed of 50 km/h or less;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference to vehicle category "L~3~" in United
      Nations Regulation No. 78 is a reference to a motorcycle that is
      designed to travel on two wheels in contact with the ground, that
      has a maximum speed of more than 50 km/h and that is not equipped
      with a sidecar;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a reference to vehicle category "L~4~" in United
      Nations Regulation No. 78 is a reference to a motorcycle that is
      designed to travel on two wheels in contact with the ground and
      that is equipped with a sidecar, and to a motorcycle that is
      designed to travel on three wheels in contact with the ground that
      are asymmetrically arranged in relation to the longitudinal median
      plane; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a reference to vehicle category "L~5~" in United
      Nations Regulation No. 78 is a reference to a motorcycle that has
      a maximum speed of more than 50 km/h and that is designed to
      travel on three wheels in contact with the ground that are
      symmetrically arranged in relation to the longitudinal median
      plane.

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section and despite the
    definition [[three-wheeled vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
    subsection 2(1) of these Regulations, the term [[three-wheeled
    vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} used in United Nations Regulation No.
    78 means a motorcycle that is designed to travel on two wheels in
    contact with the ground and that is equipped with a sidecar, and a
    motorcycle that is designed to travel on three wheels in contact
    with the ground.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph 1.1.3 of Annex 3 of
    United Nations Regulation No. 78, the peak braking coefficient shall
    be measured in accordance with paragraph 1.1.3(a) of that
    Regulation.

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph 2.4 of Annex 3 of
    United Nations Regulation No. 78, the brake temperature shall be
    measured in accordance with paragraph 2.4(b) of that Regulation.

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} Despite the second sentence of paragraph 5.1.6 of
    United Nations Regulation No. 78, sidecar wheels are never required
    to be equipped with a brake.

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} A reference to a warning lamp in paragraph 5.1.12
    of United Nations Regulation No. 78 shall be read as a reference to
    the tell-tale for a brake system malfunction set out in the table to
    section 101 of this Schedule.

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} A reference to a warning lamp in paragraph 5.1.13
    of United Nations Regulation No. 78 shall be read as a reference to
    the tell-tale for an antilock brake system malfunction set out in
    the table to section 101 of this Schedule.

  ##### [Transitional Provision]{.HTitleText3} {#transitional-provision-4 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (18), a motorcycle may,
    until September 1, 2016, conform to the requirements of this section
    as it read immediately before the day on which this subsection comes
    into force.

#### [Motorcycle Controls and Displays (Standard 123)]{.HTitleText2} {#motorcycle-controls-and-displays-standard-123 .SchedHeadL2}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/93-31, s. 4
- SOR/2007-180, s. 9
- SOR/2011-238, s. 3

\]
:::

##### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#general-6 .SchedHeadL3}

- - **123** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle, except those designed and
    sold exclusively for use by law enforcement agencies, shall conform
    to the requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 123,
    Motorcycle Controls and Displays (TSD 123), as amended from time to
    time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Instead of conforming to subsection (1),
    motorcycles, except those designed and sold exclusively for use by
    law enforcement agencies, may conform to the requirements of
    paragraph 5 of United Nations Regulation No. 60, Uniform Provisions
    Concerning the Approval of Two-Wheeled Motor Cycles and Mopeds with
    Regard to Driver-Operated Controls Including the Identification of
    Controls, Tell-Tales and Indicators (United Nations Regulation No.
    60), as amended by any amendment prior to the 01 series of
    amendments.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every motorcycle shall be equipped with a horn.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The symbols and abbreviations used to identify the
    controls and displays of a motorcycle shall be provided in the
    English and French versions of the owner's manual, accompanied by a
    full explanation.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the case of analogue speedometer displays, the
    kilometre-per-hour scale shall increase in a clockwise direction.
    Major graduations and numerals shall appear at 10 or 20 km/h
    intervals and minor graduations at 5 or 10 km/h intervals.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} An odometer or trip odometer installed on a
    motorcycle adjacent to the speedometer shall display distances in
    the same unit of measure as that of the predominant speedometer
    scale. If the odometer or trip odometer is not adjacent to the
    speedometer or switches between kilometres and miles independently
    of the speedometer, the odometer or trip odometer shall display
    distances in kilometres or miles and shall identify the unit of
    measure used.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} A turn signal lamp display fitted on a motorcycle
    shall be green or yellow.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (10), (11), (16)
    and (17), wherever the term "motorcycle" is used in International
    Standard ISO 6727, Road vehicles --- Motorcycles --- Symbols for
    controls, indicators and tell-tales (ISO 6727), as amended from time
    to time, it has the same meaning as
    [[motorcycle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1) of these
    Regulations.

  ##### [TSD 123]{.HTitleText3} {#tsd-123 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} Item 12 of Table 3 to TSD 123 applies to manual
    fuel tank shutoff valves only.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} Any control or display that is not listed in Table
    3 to TSD 123 and for which no other identification requirements are
    specified in these Regulations shall, if identified, be identified
    by the corresponding symbol set out in ISO 6727.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} Any control or display for which a symbol is not
    specified in Table 3 to TSD 123 or in ISO 6727 may be identified by
    a symbol designed by the manufacturer in compliance with the
    principles specified in ISO 6727.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} Despite the requirements of item 8 of Table 3 to
    TSD 123, a speedometer fitted on a motorcycle shall be calibrated in
    kilometres per hour or in kilometres per hour and miles per hour.
    Digital speedometers that switch between kilometres per hour and
    miles per hour are permitted if the unit of measure is identified.

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} Despite S5.2.3(c) of TSD 123, the words and
    abbreviations that are required to be displayed under this section
    shall be displayed in both official languages. However, if there is
    insufficient space to display the words or abbreviations identifying
    the ignition, tachometer or fuel tank shutoff valve in both official
    languages in a readily visible manner, they may be displayed in one
    official language only.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} S5.2.4 of TSD 123 does not apply to motor
    tricycles.

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} Despite S5.2.5 of TSD 123, it is not necessary for
    a footrest for a passenger on a motorcycle to fold rearward and
    upward when not in use.

  ##### [United Nations Regulation No. 60]{.HTitleText3} {#united-nations-regulation-no.-60 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} Despite the second paragraph of paragraph 5.1 of
    United Nations Regulation No. 60, any control, tell-tale or
    indicator for which a symbol is not identified in Table 1 of United
    Nations Regulation No. 60 and for which no other identification
    requirements are specified in these regulations, shall, if
    identified, be identified by the corresponding symbol set out in ISO
    6727.

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} Despite the second paragraph of paragraph 5.1 of
    United Nations Regulation No. 60, any control, tell-tale or
    indicator for which a symbol is not specified in Table 1 of United
    Nations Regulation No. 60 or in ISO 6727 may be identified by a
    symbol designed by the manufacturer in compliance with the
    principles specified in ISO 6727.

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph 5 of United Nations
    Regulation No. 60, if words or abbreviations are used, they shall be
    displayed in both official languages. However, if there is
    insufficient space to display the words or abbreviations identifying
    the ignition, tachometer or fuel tank shutoff valve in both official
    languages in a readily visible manner, they may be displayed in one
    official language only.

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} In addition to meeting the requirement set out in
    the column under the heading "Operation" opposite 10 in the column
    under the heading "No" of Table 1 of United Nations Regulation No.
    60, a speedometer fitted on a motorcycle shall be calibrated in
    kilometres per hour or in kilometres per hour and miles per hour.
    Digital speedometers that switch between kilometres per hour and
    miles per hour are permitted if the unit of measure is identified.

  - [(20)]{.lawlabel} Despite the second paragraphs set out in the
    column under the heading "Location" opposite each of 11 to 13 in the
    column under the heading "No" of Table 1 of United Nations
    Regulation No. 60, the controls for the audible warning device,
    driving beam and passing beam shall only comply with the
    requirements of the first paragraph of each number.

  - [(21)]{.lawlabel} Despite the third paragraph set out in the column
    under the heading "Operation" opposite 32 in the column under the
    heading "No" of Table 1 of United Nations Regulation No. 60, the
    foot selector manual gear shift control on vehicles with an engine
    capacity of less than 200 cubic centimetres shall comply with the
    requirements of the second paragraph of that column.

  - [(22)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (16) to (21):

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} references to "audible warning device",
      "driving-beam" and "passing-beam" in the English version of United
      Nations Regulation No. 60 are to be read as references to "horn",
      "upper beam" and "lower beam", respectively; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} references to "[compteur de vitesse]{.otherLang
      lang="fr"}" in the French version of United Nations Regulation No.
      60 are to be read as references to "[indicateur de
      vitesse]{.otherLang lang="fr"}".

#### [Accelerator Control Systems (Standard 124)]{.HTitleText2} {#accelerator-control-systems-standard-124 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **124** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle, truck, bus and three-wheeled vehicle shall
    conform to the requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 124,
    Accelerator Control Systems, as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/79-940, s. 7
  - SOR/2007-180, s. 10
  - SOR/2011-238, s. 3
  - SOR/2014-307, s. 10
  :::

#### [Electronic Stability Control Systems for Light Vehicles (Standard 126)]{.HTitleText2} {#electronic-stability-control-systems-for-light-vehicles-standard-126 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **126** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle, truck and bus with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less
    must conform to the requirements of Technical Standards Document No.
    126, Electronic Stability Control Systems for Light Vehicles (TSD
    126), as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Instead of conforming to the requirements of
    subsection (1), every passenger car, multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle, truck and bus with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less, may comply
    with the requirements set out in Annex 9 of United Nations
    Regulation No. 13H, Uniform provisions concerning the approval of
    passenger cars with regard to braking, as amended by any amendment
    prior to the 01 series of amendments, with the following
    adaptations:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 3.3.1 of that Annex is to be read as
      allowing the lateral displacement to only be calculated using the
      double integration formula;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 3.5.1.1 of that Annex is to be read as
      requiring that the additional gear reduction be of at least 2.0;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 4.1.1 of that Annex is to be read as
      requiring the ambient temperature to be between 7°C and 40°C;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 4.2.2.2 of that Annex does not apply;
      and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the tires referred to in paragraph 4.3.3 of that
      Annex shall be the tires installed on the vehicle before it is
      sold to the first retail purchaser.

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/2013-55, s. 1
  - SOR/2014-307, s. 11
  - SOR/2017-57, s. 4
  - SOR/2017-104, s. 6
  :::

#### [School Bus Pedestrian Safety Devices (Standard 131)]{.HTitleText2} {#school-bus-pedestrian-safety-devices-standard-131 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#application .SchedHeadL3}

- - **131** [(0.1)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to school buses
    other than multifunction school activity buses.

  ##### [Stop Signals]{.HTitleText3} {#stop-signals .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every school bus must be equipped with one or two
    stop signal arms that conform to the requirements of Technical
    Standards Document No. 131, School Bus Pedestrian Safety Devices
    (TSD 131), as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The word "[ARRÊT]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" may appear
    instead of, or together with, the word "STOP" in the manner
    specified with respect to the word "STOP" in S5.2.2 of TSD 131.

  ##### [Image Recording System]{.HTitleText3} {#image-recording-system .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the school bus is equipped with a system that is
    intended to record images of a vehicle that is passing the school
    bus when the red signal warning lamps referred to in Table I-a of
    Technical Standards Document No. 108, Lamps, Reflective Devices, and
    Associated Equipment are activated, the system must

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be placed in a manner that allows the system to
      record images of the vehicle's rear licence plate;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} record the images automatically; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} operate in temperatures from −40°C to 40°C.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} Whether or not the school bus is equipped with the
    system referred to in subsection (3), a label, as shown in Figure 1,
    must be permanently affixed to the school bus so that the label is
    readily visible to a driver who is behind the school bus.

    <figure>
    <div class="imageGroupcenter">
    <img src="/images/C.R.C.,_c._1038_e_graphics/C.R.C.,_c._1038_e_016.jpg"
    alt="White rectangle with black border, text, lines and symbols. At the top of the rectangle above a horizontal line: a triangle with an exclamation mark centered in it and, to the right of the triangle, the word “ATTENTION”. Below the horizontal line: a camera to the left of a vertical line and, to the right of the vertical line, in brackets, the words “Bilingual statement warning that this school bus may be equipped with a camera”." />
    </div>
    <figcaption><p>Figure 1</p></figcaption>
    </figure>

  ##### [Transitional Provision]{.HTitleText3} {#transitional-provision-5 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Until November 1, 2027, a school bus referred to in
    subsection (0.1) may conform to the requirements of this section as
    it read on the day before the day on which this subsection comes
    into force.

#### [Light Vehicle Brake Systems (Standard 135)]{.HTitleText2} {#light-vehicle-brake-systems-standard-135 .SchedHeadL2}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2001-35, s. 5
- SOR/2011-238, s. 3

\]
:::

- - **135** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, every three-wheeled
    vehicle and every multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck and bus
    with a GVWR of 3 500 kg or less shall conform to the requirements of
    Technical Standards Document No. 135, Light Vehicle Brake Systems
    (TSD 135), as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The statement set out in S5.4.3 of TSD 135 may be
    replaced by another statement to the same effect.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} A brake warning indicator referred to in S5.5.5 of
    TSD 135 shall, when activated due to a condition set out in S5.5.1
    of TSD 135, display the identification symbol set out in the table
    to section 101 of this Schedule that corresponds to that condition,
    but if the vehicle is fitted with a single common brake warning
    indicator, the indicator shall display the identification symbol for
    a brake system malfunction set out in the table to section 101 of
    this Schedule.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The word "car" used in S6.3.6 and S6.3.7 of the
    English version of TSD 135 shall be read as "vehicle".

  - [(5) to (7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2015-24, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Electronic Stability Control Systems for Heavy Vehicles (CMVSS 136)]{.HTitleText2} {#electronic-stability-control-systems-for-heavy-vehicles-cmvss-136 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **136** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), truck tractors
    and buses referred to in S3 of the standard set out in subpart 136,
    part 571, chapter V, Title 49 of the Code of Federal Regulations of
    the United States (FMVSS 136), must comply with the requirements of
    that standard, as amended from time to time. However,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the definition of [[over-the-road
      bus]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} set out in the standard does not
      apply;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the terms [[perimeter-seating
      bus]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[transit
      bus]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in the standard have the meaning as in
      subsection 2(1) of these Regulations;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the electronic stability control system
      malfunction tell-tale for vehicles subject to CMVSS 136 must be
      identified by the symbol set out in the table to section 101 of
      this Schedule or by the abbreviation "ESC"; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} "National Highway Traffic Safety Administration"
      in the standard is to be read as "Transport Canada".

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite FMVSS 136, subsection (1) applies to school
    buses.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to vehicles manufactured on or
    after the dates set out in FMVSS 136.

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/2017-104, s. 7
  :::

#### [Minimum Sound Requirements for Hybrid and Electric Vehicles (CMVSS 141)]{.HTitleText2} {#minimum-sound-requirements-for-hybrid-and-electric-vehicles-cmvss-141 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **141** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every hybrid or electric passenger car,
    multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck, bus and low-speed vehicle
    with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less shall conform to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements set out in paragraph 6 of
      Regulation No 138 of the Economic Commission for Europe of the
      United Nations, entitled Uniform provisions concerning the
      approval of Quiet Road Transport Vehicles with regard to their
      reduced audibility, as amended from time to time, and shall be
      tested in accordance with the conditions and test procedures set
      out in Annex 3 of that Regulation, as amended from time to time;
      or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the requirements set out in provision S5 of
      section 571.141, subpart B, part 571, chapter V, Title 49 of the
      Code of Federal Regulations of the United States, entitled
      Standard No. 141; Minimum Sound Requirements for Hybrid and
      Electric Vehicles, as amended from time to time, and shall be
      tested in accordance with the conditions and test procedures set
      out in provisions S6 and S7 of that section, as amended from time
      to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs 1(a) and (b),
    paragraph 2 of Regulation No 138 and provision S4 of Standard No.
    141, as amended from time to time, apply to the interpretation of
    those documents, respectively.

### [PART III]{.HLabel1} {#part-iii .SchedHeadL1}

- **200** [\[Repealed, SOR/83-138, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Occupant Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#occupant-protection .SchedHeadL2}

- - **201** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section,

    [[head impact area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[head impact area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the non-glazed
        surfaces of the interior of the vehicle that are capable of
        being contacted statically by the head form of a measuring
        device in accordance with the following procedure or its graphic
        equivalent:

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at each designated seating position, by
          placing the pivot point of the measuring device,

          - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for seats that are adjustable fore and aft,
            at

            - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the seating reference point, and

            - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a point 130 mm (five inches) horizontally
              forward of the seating reference point displaced
              vertically 20 mm (0.75 inches) or a distance equal to the
              rise that results from a 130 mm (five inches) forward
              adjustment of the seat, and

          - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for seats that are not adjustable fore and
            aft, at the seating reference point,

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the pivot point to top-of-head dimension
          at each adjustment allowed for the measuring device by the
          interior dimension of the vehicle, by determining all contact
          points above the lower windshield glass line and forward of
          the seating reference point, and

        - [(c)]{.lawlabel} beginning with the head form of the measuring
          device at each contact point referred to in paragraph (b) and
          with the measuring device in a vertical position if no contact
          point exists for a particular adjusted length, by pivoting the
          measuring device forward and downward through all arcs in
          vertical planes to 90° each side of the longitudinal plane
          through the seating reference point until the head form
          contacts an interior surface or until it is tangent to a
          horizontal plane 25 mm (1 inch) above the seating reference
          point, whichever occurs first; ([zone d'impact de la
          tête]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[interior compartment door]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[interior compartment door]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any door
        in the interior of a vehicle installed by the manufacturer as a
        cover for storage space normally used for personal
        effects; ([porte de compartiment intérieur]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

    [[measuring device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[measuring device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device having a
        spherical head form with a diameter of 165 mm (6.5 inches)
        attached to a pivot, with the distance between the pivot point
        and the top of the head form being adjustable at any point
        between 740 mm (29 inches) and 840 mm (33 inches); ([appareil de
        mesure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[pelvic impact area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[pelvic impact area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means that area of
        the door or body side panel adjacent to any outboard designated
        seating position that is bounded by:

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} horizontal planes 180 mm (seven inches) above
          and 100 mm (four inches) below the seating reference point,
          and

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vertical transverse planes 200 mm (eight
          inches) forward and 50 mm (two inches) rearward of the seating
          reference point. ([zone d'impact pelvienne]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to passenger cars and to
    multi-purpose passenger vehicles, trucks and buses with a GVWR of
    4 536 kg (10,000 pounds) or less.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3.2), the instrument panel
    of a vehicle shall be constructed so that the deceleration of a
    spherical head form with a weight of 6,8 kg (15 pounds) and a
    diameter of 165 mm (6.5 inches) does not exceed 80 g continuously
    for more than 3 ms when the area of the instrument panel that is
    within the head impact area is impacted in accordance with the test
    procedure referred to in subsection (3.1) at a relative velocity of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 19.3 km/h, in the case of a vehicle that is
      equipped, at the right front outboard designated seating position,
      with an air bag and a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that
      conform to section 208; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 24 km/h (15 mph), in the case of any other
      vehicle.

  - [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The test of the instrument panel shall be
    performed in accordance with SAE Recommended Practice, "Motor
    Vehicle Instrument Panel Laboratory Impact Test Procedure --- Head
    Area --- SAE J921b" (November 1971) and using the instrumentation
    whose performance is specified in SAE Recommended Practice,
    "Instrumentation For Impact Tests --- SAE J211" (October 1988)
    except that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the origin of the line tangent to the surface of
      the instrument panel shall be a point on a transverse horizontal
      line through a point 130 mm (5 inches) horizontally forward of the
      seating reference point of the front outboard designated seating
      position, displaced vertically 19 mm (0.75 inches) or a distance
      equal to the rise in the seating reference point that results from
      a 130 mm (5 inch) forward adjustment of the seat; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the impact shall be directed in a vertical plane
      parallel to the longitudinal axis of the vehicle or in a plane
      normal to the surface at the point of contact.

  - [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} console assemblies;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} areas less than 130 mm (5 inches) from the
      junction of the instrument panel and the inside of the side
      structure of the body;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} areas closer to the junction of the windshield
      and the instrument panel than those areas that are capable of
      being contacted by the head form with the windshield in place;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} areas outboard of all points on the line of
      contact of the head form with the instrument panel when the head
      form is moved along the inboard side of a vertical longitudinal
      plane tangent to the inboard edge of the steering wheel; or

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} areas below any point at which a vertical line is
      tangent to the rearmost surface of the instrument panel.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4.1), the seats of a vehicle
    shall be constructed so that the deceleration of a spherical head
    form with a weight of 6,8 kg (15 pounds) and a diameter of 165 mm
    (6.5 inches) does not exceed 80 g continuously for more than 3 ms
    when the area of a seatback that is within the head impact area is
    impacted, at a relative velocity of 24 km/h (15 miles per hour), in
    accordance with SAE Recommended Practice, "Motor Vehicle Seatback
    Assembly Laboratory Impact Test Procedure --- Head Area --- SAE
    J346" (June 1971) using the instrumentation whose performance is
    specified in SAE Recommended Practice, "Instrumentation For Impact
    Tests --- SAE J211" (October 1988), except that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the origin of the line tangent to the uppermost
      component of the seatback frame shall be a point on a transverse
      horizontal line through the seating reference point of the
      appropriate rear outboard designated seating position, with
      adjustable forward seats in their rearmost design driving position
      and reclinable forward seatbacks in their normal design position;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the impact shall be directed in a vertical plane
      parallel to the longitudinal axis of the vehicle or in a plane
      normal to the surface at the point of contact;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a seat without head restraints, each test shall
        be performed

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each individual split seatback
          or bucket seat seatback, between two points each situated
          100 mm (4 inches) outboard of its centreline, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each bench seat seatback,
          between two points each situated 100 mm (4 inches) outboard of
          the centreline of each outboard designated seating position,
          and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a seat with head restraints, each test shall
        be conducted at a point on the centreline of each head restraint
        with the head restraint in place at its lowest adjusted
        position; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type of seat that is installed
      in more than one body style, tests conducted at the foremost and
      rearmost positions identified by the application of paragraph (a)
      shall be deemed to be conclusive for all intermediate positions.

  - [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply in respect of

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} seats installed in a school bus that complies
      with section 222 when tested in accordance with Motor Vehicle
      Safety Test Methods, section 222, "School Bus Passenger Seating
      and Crash Protection" (December 19, 1983);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rearmost seats;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} side-facing seats;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} back-to-back seats;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} folding auxiliary jump seats; and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} temporary seats.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (7), every interior
    compartment door located in an instrument panel, console, seatback
    or side panel adjacent to an outboard designated seating position
    shall remain closed when

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the interior compartment door latch system is
      tested by being subjected to a force equal to an inertia load of
      10 g in a horizontal transverse direction and an inertia load of
      10 g in a vertical direction, in accordance with SAE Recommended
      Practice, "Passenger Car Side Door Latch Systems --- SAE J839"
      (July 1982);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one of the following tests is conducted, namely,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle is tested by being impacted
        perpendicularly into a fixed collision barrier at a forward
        longitudinal velocity of 48 km/h (30 miles per hour), or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the interior compartment door latch system is
        tested by being subjected to a force equal to a horizontal
        inertia load of 30 g in a longitudinal direction, in accordance
        with SAE Recommended Practice, "Passenger Car Side Door Latch
        Systems --- SAE J839" (July 1982); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the instrument panel or seatback is tested in
      accordance with subsection (3) or (4), as the case may be.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/92-689, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} All interior compartment door assemblies that have
    a locking device shall be tested with the locking device in an
    unlocked position.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} A sun visor shall be provided at each front
    outboard designated seating position on every vehicle and shall be
    constructed of, or covered with, energy absorbing material and so
    mounted that each mounting shall not have a rigid material edge with
    a radius of less than 3 mm (0.125 inch) that is statically
    contactable by a spherical head form with a diameter of 165 mm (6.5
    inches).

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} Each arm-rest installed on a vehicle, other than an
    arm-rest referred to in subsection (10), shall meet at least one of
    the following performance requirements:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be constructed of energy absorbing material that
      collapses laterally at least 50 mm (2 inches) without permitting
      contact with any underlying rigid material;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be constructed

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} of energy absorbing material that collapses to
        within 32 mm (1.25 inches) of a rigid test panel surface without
        permitting contact with any rigid material, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} so that the vertical height of any rigid
        material between 13 mm (0.5 inches) and 32 mm (1.25 inches) from
        the panel surface is not less than 25 mm (1 inch); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when measured vertically in side elevation,
      provide at least 50 mm (2 inches) of coverage within the pelvic
      impact area along not less than 50 mm (2 inches) of its length.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} Each arm-rest that folds into the seat back or
    between two seat backs of a vehicle shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} comply with subsection (9); or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be constructed of or covered with energy
      absorbing material.

#### [Head Restraints]{.HTitleText2} {#head-restraints .SchedHeadL2}

- **202** Every multi-purpose passenger vehicle, bus or truck with a
  GVWR of 4 536 kg or less, every passenger car and every three-wheeled
  vehicle shall conform to the requirements of Technical Standards
  Document No. 202, Head Restraints (TSD 202), as amended from time to
  time.

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/95-60, s. 2
  - SOR/2003-272, s. 21

#### [Driver Impact Protection and Steering Control System]{.HTitleText2} {#driver-impact-protection-and-steering-control-system .SchedHeadL2}

- - **203** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The steering control system of a vehicle
    shall be constructed in such a manner that no component or
    attachment --- including any horn actuating mechanism --- is
    capable, during normal driving manoeuvres, of catching the driver's
    clothing or the driver's jewellery, such as a watch, a ring or a
    bracelet other than a bracelet with loosely attached or dangling
    members.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the steering control
    system of every passenger car and three-wheeled vehicle and every
    multi-purpose passenger vehicle, bus and truck --- other than a
    walk-in van --- with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less shall be tested in
    accordance with SAE Recommended Practice J944, Steering Control
    System --- Passenger Car --- Laboratory Test Procedure (June 1980).

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following requirements with respect to the
    steering control system shall be met during the test referred to in
    subsection (2):

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the system shall be impacted by a body block at a
      relative velocity of 24 km/h;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the impact force developed on the chest of the
      body block and transmitted to the system shall not exceed
      11 120 N, except for intervals with a cumulative duration of not
      more than 3 ms.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) do not apply to a vehicle
    that conforms to the requirements of S5 and S14 of Technical
    Standards Document No. 208, Occupant Crash Protection (TSD 208), as
    amended from time to time.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Steering Column Rearward Displacement]{.HTitleText2} {#steering-column-rearward-displacement .SchedHeadL2}

- - **204** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car and three-wheeled
    vehicle, and every truck, bus and multi-purpose passenger vehicle
    --- other than a walk-in van --- with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less and
    an unloaded vehicle mass of 2 495 kg or less, shall conform to the
    requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 204, Steering
    Control Rearward Displacement (TSD 204), as amended from time to
    time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the words
    [[passenger car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} used in TSD 204 mean
    [[passenger car and three-wheeled vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a vehicle that
    conforms to the requirements of S5 and S14 of Technical Standards
    Document No. 208, Occupant Crash Protection (TSD 208), as amended
    from time to time.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Glazing Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#glazing-materials .SchedHeadL2}

- - **205** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
    [[bus]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[motorhome]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
    [[multi-purpose passenger vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
    [[passenger car]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[readily removable
    windows]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[trailer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
    and [[truck]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard
    --- 1996 shall have the same meaning as in subsection 2(1) of these
    Regulations.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the definition
    [[motor vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in the ANSI Z26 Safety
    Standard --- 1996 does not apply.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) to (13), each piece of
    glazing material for use in a vehicle, except a trailer other than a
    bus trailer, shall meet the requirements of the ANSI Z26 Safety
    Standard --- 1996.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency or conflict
    between the requirements for items 1 to 16B listed in section 4.2 of
    the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996 and the requirements shown in
    Table 1 of that Standard, the latter shall prevail.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any glazing material for use in a multi-purpose
    passenger vehicle shall meet the requirements specified in the ANSI
    Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996 for glazing material for use in trucks.

  - [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} Any glazing material for use in a three-wheeled
    vehicle shall meet the requirements specified in the ANSI Z26 Safety
    Standard --- 1996 for glazing materials for use in passenger cars.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 6 of the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard
    --- 1996, the exposed edges of tempered glazing material installed
    in school buses need not be banded.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} Instead of using the procedures described in the
    ANSI Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996 for tests 1 and 16 described in
    that Standard, those tests may be conducted using the procedures
    described for tests 1 and 16 in the ANSI Z26 Safety Code --- 1990.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} Instead of using the steel frame made in accordance
    with Figure 1 in the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996 required for
    tests 6, 8 to 14, 26 and 29 described in that Standard, those tests
    may be conducted using a maple or similar hardwood frame made in
    accordance with Figure 1 in the ANSI Z26 Safety Code --- 1990.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} Instead of conducting the fracture test using the
    procedure for test 7 described in the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard ---
    1996 and testing the specimens specified in that test, the fracture
    test may be conducted using the following procedure:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} test the uncracked and unbroken specimens
      remaining from test 6 described in the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard
      --- 1996;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continue the procedure for test 6 by increasing
      the height from which the sphere is dropped by increments of
      305 mm starting from a height of 3.35 m until each specimen is
      broken; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} interpret the results in accordance with test 7.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} When conducting tests 9 and 12 described in the
    ANSI Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996, the specimens of the glazing
    materials listed in item 14 of section 4 of the ANSI Z26 Safety
    Standard --- 1996 may be clamped in the frame of the holding
    fixture.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} When conducting test 26 described in the ANSI Z26
    Safety Standard --- 1996, the specimens of the glazing materials
    listed in item 14 of section 4 of the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard ---
    1996 shall be clamped in the frame of the holding fixture.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} Any plastic glazing material that meets the
    requirements of item 7 of section 4 of the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard
    --- 1996 may be used in that portion of a motorcycle windshield that
    is below an imaginary horizontal plane located 380 mm vertically
    above the lowest seating position.

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} Each piece of glazing material installed in a
    vehicle shall be legibly and permanently marked with

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the appropriate marking required by section 7 of
      the ANSI Z26 Safety Standard --- 1996; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an approved code mark, assigned by the Minister
      to the glazing material manufacturer, in letters and numbers not
      less than 1.8 mm in height.

#### [Door Locks and Door Retention Components (Standard 206)]{.HTitleText2} {#door-locks-and-door-retention-components-standard-206 .SchedHeadL2}

- - **206** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every bus with a GVWR of 4 536 kg or less,
    enclosed motorcycle, multi-purpose passenger vehicle, three-wheeled
    vehicle, passenger car and truck that is equipped with side doors or
    back doors shall be so equipped in accordance with either

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements of Technical Standards Document
      No. 206, Door Locks and Door Retention Components (TSD 206), as
      amended from time to time; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the general requirements, performance
      requirements and test procedures set out in United Nations
      Regulation No. 11, Uniform Provisions Concerning the Approval of
      Vehicles with regard to Door Latches and Door Retention Components
      (United Nations Regulation No. 11), as amended from time to time
      by any amendment in the 03 or 04 series of amendments.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph 6.3.2 of United
    Nations Regulation No. 11, the locking device shall be the device
    referred to in paragraph 6.3.2.1(a) or (b) of that Regulation.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 16\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Anchorage of Seats]{.HTitleText2} {#anchorage-of-seats .SchedHeadL2}

- - **207** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, three-wheeled vehicle,
    truck, bus and multi-purpose passenger vehicle shall conform to the
    requirements of Technical Standards Document No. 207, Anchorage of
    Seats (TSD 207), as amended from time to time.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The label referred to in S4.4 of TSD 207 shall be
    in both official languages.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a truck or multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle with a GVWR greater than 4 536 kg or a motor home, a seat
    that is labelled in accordance with S4.4 of TSD 207 is not
    considered to be a designated seating position.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

  ::: HistoricalNote
  - SOR/2011-264, s. 5
  - SOR/2014-307, s. 17
  - SOR/2016-318, s. 9
  :::

#### [Occupant Protection In Frontal Impacts (Standard 208)]{.HTitleText2} {#occupant-protection-in-frontal-impacts-standard-208 .SchedHeadL2}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2013-9, s. 5

\]
:::

##### [Seat Belt Installation Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#seat-belt-installation-requirements .SchedHeadL3}

- - **208** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every enclosed motorcycle shall be equipped
    at each designated seating position with a Type 2 manual seat belt
    assembly that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
      detached from the pelvic restraint;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an emergency-locking
      retractor; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car and three-wheeled vehicle, and
    every truck and multi-purpose passenger vehicle with a GVWR of 4 536
    kg or less, shall be equipped

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at each front outboard designated seating
      position except the one referred to in paragraph (b), and at each
      rear designated seating position except those referred to in
      paragraphs (c) and (d), with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly
      that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
        detached from the pelvic restraint,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
        emergency-locking retractor, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at each front outboard designated seating
      position that is designed for a disabled person, with a Type 2
      seat belt assembly;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a seat designed to be easily removed and replaced by means of
      equipment installed by a manufacturer for that purpose, or that is
      adjacent to a walkway located between the seat and the side of the
      vehicle and designed to allow access to more rearward seating
      positions, or that is an inboard designated seating position that
      has a seat whose back can be folded so that no part of the back
      extends above a horizontal plane located 250 mm above the highest
      seating reference point on the seat, with a Type 2 manual seat
      belt assembly that conforms to the requirements of paragraph (a)
      or with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} can be detached from the upper or lower
        anchorage point, but not from both, by means of a key or
        key-like object,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
        emergency-locking retractor, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
        detached from the pelvic restraint;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a seat that can be adjusted to change the direction it is facing,
      with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that conforms to the
      requirements of paragraph (a) and can function regardless of the
      direction the seat is adjusted to face, or with a Type 2 manual
      seat belt assembly that cannot be detached from any anchorage
      point and that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has a pelvic restraint that restrains the
        movement of the pelvis regardless of the direction the seat is
        adjusted to face and is equipped with an emergency-locking
        retractor, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a seat that can be placed in a
        forward-facing or rear-facing position or within ±30° of either
        position, has an upper torso restraint that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be detached from the pelvic restraint,

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor,

        - [(C)]{.lawlabel} is for use only in conjunction with the
          pelvic restraint, and

        - [(D)]{.lawlabel} can function when the seat is in any position
          in which it can be placed within that range; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a side-facing seat, and at each front inboard designated seating
      position,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that
        conforms to the requirements of paragraph (a),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has a pelvic restraint that can be adjusted
          by means of an emergency-locking retractor, an
          automatic-locking retractor or a manual adjusting device,

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that can be
          adjusted by means of an emergency-locking retractor or a
          manual adjusting device, and

        - [(C)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point,
          or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 1 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor, an automatic-locking retractor or
          a manual adjusting device, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every truck and multi-purpose passenger vehicle
    with a GVWR greater than 4 536 kg shall be equipped

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at each front outboard designated seating
      position

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
          detached from the pelvic restraint,

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor or an automatic-locking retractor,
          and

        - [(C)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point,
          or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 1 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor or an automatic-locking retractor,
          and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point;
          and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at each front inboard designated seating position
      and at each rear designated seating position

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
          detached from the pelvic restraint,

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor, an automatic-locking retractor or
          a manual adjusting device, and

        - [(C)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point,
          or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 1 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor, an automatic-locking retractor or
          a manual adjusting device, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a motor home, the number of
    designated seating positions with seat belts shall not be less than
    the number of sleeping positions.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every bus, other than a school bus, transit bus,
    perimeter-seating bus and prison bus, must be equipped

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at each designated seating position, except those
      referred to in paragraphs (b) to (d), with a Type 2 manual seat
      belt assembly that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
        detached from the pelvic restraint,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
        emergency-locking retractor, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a seat designed to be easily removed and replaced by means of
      equipment installed by a manufacturer for that purpose, or that is
      adjacent to a walkway located between the seat and the side of the
      vehicle and designed to allow access to more rearward seating
      positions, or that is an inboard designated seating position that
      has a seat whose back can be folded so that no part of the back
      extends above a horizontal plane located 250 mm above the highest
      seating reference point on the seat, with a Type 2 manual seat
      belt assembly that conforms to the requirements of paragraph (a)
      or with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} can be detached from the upper or lower
        anchorage point, but not from both, by means of a key or
        key-like object,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
        emergency-locking retractor, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
        detached from the pelvic restraint;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a seat that can be adjusted to change the direction it is facing,
      with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that conforms to the
      requirements of paragraph (a) and can function regardless of the
      direction the seat is adjusted to face, or with a Type 2 manual
      seat belt assembly that cannot be detached from any anchorage
      point and that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has a pelvic restraint that restrains the
        movement of the pelvis regardless of the direction the seat is
        adjusted to face and is equipped with an emergency-locking
        retractor, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a seat that can be placed in a
        forward-facing or rear-facing position or within ±30° of either
        position, an upper torso restraint that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be detached from the pelvic restraint,

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor,

        - [(C)]{.lawlabel} is for use only in conjunction with the
          pelvic restraint, and

        - [(D)]{.lawlabel} can function when the seat is in any position
          in which it can be placed within that range; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} at each rear designated seating position that has
      a side-facing seat,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that
        conforms to the requirements of paragraph (a),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has a pelvic restraint that can be adjusted
          by means of an emergency-locking retractor, an
          automatic-locking retractor or a manual adjusting device, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from the pelvic restraint
          or from any anchorage point, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} with a Type 1 manual seat belt assembly that

        - [(A)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an
          emergency-locking retractor, an automatic-locking retractor or
          a manual adjusting device, and

        - [(B)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every school bus, transit bus, perimeter-seating
    bus and prison bus must be equipped, at the driver's designated
    seating position, with a Type 2 manual seat belt assembly that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has an upper torso restraint that cannot be
      detached from the pelvic restraint;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} can be adjusted by means of an emergency-locking
      retractor or an automatic-locking retractor; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cannot be detached from any anchorage point.

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2018-143-2, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} \[Reserved\]

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} An automatic-locking retractor that is installed in
    order for a seat belt assembly to conform to the requirements of
    paragraph (2)(e), (3)(a) or (b) or (5)(d) or subsection (6) shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} engage the next locking position when a length of
      seat belt webbing between 19 mm and 77 mm has moved into the
      retractor, as measured from an initial position determined by
      extending the seat belt webbing to 75 per cent of its total length
      from the retractor; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if used on a vehicle seat that has a suspension
      system, be attached to the suspended portion of the seat.

  ##### [Seat Belt Fit]{.HTitleText3} {#seat-belt-fit .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} A Type 2 manual seat belt assembly shall be
    constructed so that when a 50th percentile adult male occupant is
    secured in place by the seat belt assembly, the intersection of the
    upper torso restraint and the pelvic restraint shall be at least 150
    mm from the front vertical centreline of the occupant, measured
    along the centreline of the pelvic restraint, with

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any upper torso restraint manual adjusting device
      adjusted in accordance with the manufacturer's instructions;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle seat adjusted to its rearmost and
      lowest position; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the seat back adjusted to the manufacturer's
      nominal design riding position.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} When the vehicle seat is placed in any position,
    and the seat back is placed in the manufacturer's nominal design
    riding position and any adjustable seat belt anchorage is placed in
    the manufacturer's nominal design position for a 50th percentile
    adult male occupant, every pelvic restraint shall

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the driver's designated seating position, be
      adjustable to fit any occupant whose dimensions range from those
      of a 5th percentile adult female to those of a 95th percentile
      adult male; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at all of the other designated seating positions,
      be adjustable to fit any occupant whose dimensions range from
      those of a 50th percentile six-year-old child to those of a 95th
      percentile adult male.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} When the vehicle seat is placed in any position,
    and the seat back is placed in the manufacturer's nominal design
    riding position and any adjustable seat belt anchorage is placed in
    the manufacturer's nominal design position for a 50th percentile
    adult male occupant, every upper torso restraint shall be adjustable
    to fit any occupant whose dimensions range from those of a 5th
    percentile adult female to those of a 95th percentile adult male.

  ##### [Technical Standards Document No. 208]{.HTitleText3} {#technical-standards-document-no.-208 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} Every passenger car, multi-purpose passenger
    vehicle, truck, bus and three-wheeled vehicle, and their owner's
    manuals, shall conform to the requirements of Technical Standards
    Document No. 208, Occupant Crash Protection (TSD 208), as amended
    from time to time.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words [[passenger car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
      used in TSD 208 mean [[passenger car and three-wheeled
      vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the word [[dummy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} used in
      the English version of TSD 208 means [[anthropomorphic test
      device]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}.

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (13), every vehicle with a
    front outboard designated seating position that is designed for a
    disabled person may, instead of conforming to the requirements of
    S5, S7.1, S7.2, S7.4 and S14 to S27 of TSD 208, display the
    following statement on one or more labels, permanently affixed in
    view of the occupants of the front designated seating positions, in
    letters of not less than six points in height: "The \[here refer to
    the outboard designated seating position in the front row of
    designated seating positions that does not conform to the
    requirements set out in CMVSS 208\] does not conform to all of the
    requirements set out in CMVSS 208. / [La \[insérer ici la place
    assise désignée extérieure de la première rangée de places assises
    designées qui n'est pas conforme aux exigences prévues par la NSVAC
    208\] n'est pas conforme à toutes les exigences prévues par la NSVAC
    208]{.otherLang lang="fr"}."

  - [(16)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (13), every three-wheeled
    vehicle shall, at the option of the manufacturer, either conform to
    the requirements of S5 and S14 to S27 of TSD 208 or display the
    following statement on one or more labels, permanently affixed in
    view of the occupants of the front designated seating positions, in
    letters of not less than six points in height: "This vehicle does
    not conform to the requirements of the dynamic or static tests set
    out in CMVSS 208. / [Ce véhicule n'est pas conforme aux exigences
    des essais dynamiques ou statiques prévues par la NSVAC
    208]{.otherLang lang="fr"}."

  - [(17)]{.lawlabel} If a label referred to in subsection (15) or (16)
    is displayed in a vehicle, the English and French versions of the
    owner's manual shall include the statement contained on the label.

  - [(18)]{.lawlabel} The information contained on the label referred to
    in S4.5.1(a) of TSD 208 shall be in both official languages.

  - [(19)]{.lawlabel} Despite S4.5.4 of TSD 208, a passenger car,
    three-wheeled vehicle, multi-purpose passenger vehicle, truck and
    bus manufactured on or after September 1, 2012 may be equipped with
    a device that deactivates the air bag installed at the right front
    outboard designated seating position in the vehicle if all of the
    conditions in S4.5.4.1 to S4.5.4.4 of that TSD are satisfied.

  - [(20)]{.lawlabel} Despite S6.4(b) of TSD 208, the compression
    deflection of the sternum relative to the spine of the upper thorax
    of each anthropomorphic test device shall not exceed 55 mm.

  - [(21)]{.lawlabel} The information referred to in S4.5, S7.1.1.5 and
    S7.4.2 of TSD 208 shall be provided in the English and French
    versions of the owner's manual.

  - [(22)]{.lawlabel} S14 of TSD 208 applies to every passenger car, and
    to every truck, bus and multi-purpose passenger vehicle --- other
    than a walk-in van --- with a GVWR of 3 856 kg or less and an
    unloaded vehicle weight of 2 495 kg or less.

  - [(23)]{.lawlabel} Despite S15.3.4 of TSD 208, the compression
    deflection of the sternum relative to the spine of the upper thorax
    of each anthropomorphic test device shall not exceed 45 mm, when the
    vehicle is tested in accordance with S16.1(a)(2) or S18 of TSD 208.

  - [(24)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2014-307, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

  ##### [Air Bag Warning Labels]{.HTitleText3} {#air-bag-warning-labels .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(25)]{.lawlabel} If a vehicle is equipped with an air bag at a
    front outboard designated seating position, the vehicle shall have a
    label or labels permanently affixed to the sun visor at that
    designated seating position or permanently affixed in a readily
    visible area adjacent to the sun visor stating, in letters of not
    less than six points in height, in both official languages, the
    following warnings:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a right front outboard designated
      seating position with an air bag that can be deactivated by means
      of a manual cut-off switch,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a warning not to install an infant restraint
        system or a rear-facing child restraint system in that
        designated seating position unless the air bag is deactivated,
        and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a warning about the safety of children around
        air bags; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, a warning about the safety of
      children around air bags.

  ##### [Transitional Provision]{.HTitleText3} {#transitional-provision-6 .SchedHeadL3}

  - [(26)]{.lawlabel} Until September 1, 2020, buses may conform to the
    applicable requirements of this section as it read on the day before
    the day on which this subsection came into force.
